# Phase 4.0, "Foundations and the first WASM program", Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** `nova build --target wasm`, and its alias `nova bundle`, turn a
program into `dist/`: `index.html`, `app.js` and `app.wasm`, which run in a
browser or under Node. A program may use the whole language and std's core,
bytes, collections, strings, fmt and json. The runtime is `std/rt`, written
in Nova on nine memory builtins, and the shim does printing, `Float` text
and case mapping, each held to `nova-runtime`'s results. `nova check
--target wasm` checks against WASM's std and rules. The gate: hello world
and 46 `tests/runtime/` programs behave under Node as their native builds
do, on all three systems, and hello world is under 30 KB gzipped.

**Architecture:**
- **`nova-resolver`:** `CompileTarget` (`Native`, `Wasm`), with each
  target's std modules; `WASM_STD_MODULES` (six of today's, then
  `$std.rt`); nine `Builtin`s seeded only into a WASM build's std
  (`Builtin::WASM_ONLY`); E0090 for an `extern` block on WASM;
  `resolve_program_for`.
- **`nova-std`:** `std/rt/lib.nova`, as `nova_std::RT`, outside `ALL`.
- **`nova-typeck`:** the nine builtins' signatures.
- **`nova-mir`:** `Stmt::Mem` and `MemOp` (the builtins in line);
  `RtFunc::on_wasm()` and `WasmRuntime` (each runtime function's kind on
  WASM); `RuntimeImpls`, `Options` and `lower_module_with`, which route a
  runtime call to `std/rt`'s function and report E0091;
  `Module::runtime_alloc`; `BinOp` re-exported.
- **`nova-codegen-wasm`:** `compile_module` (MIR to one module with
  `wasm-encoder`), `structure.rs` (Ramsey's structuring, with a dispatch
  loop for an irreducible graph), `shim` and `INDEX_HTML` (`app.js` from
  the module's imports; `shim/*.js`).
- **`nova-driver`:** `Target`, `check_program_for`,
  `check_program_counted_for`, `build_program_wasm`; the runtime table
  from `$std.rt`'s functions.
- **`nova-cli`:** `build --target`, `bundle`, `check --target`.
- **The release set:** `nova-codegen-wasm` published, 20 crates.
- **Records:** ADRs 0038 and 0039, and dated notes.

**Tech Stack:**
- Rust: MSRV 1.78, edition 2021.
- **New crates, approved with the spec on 2026-10-14 (spec §8):**
  `wasm-encoder = "=0.243.0"`, which brings `leb128fmt` 0.1.0, and
  `wasmparser = "=0.243.0"` as a dev-dependency, without default features.
- **`Cargo.lock` changes by 30 lines:** the 29 spec §2 measured (Task 2),
  and one in Task 5, where `nova-driver`'s list gains `nova-codegen-wasm`,
  the dependency spec §8 names. See decision 1.
- JS for the shim: ES modules, run by browsers and Node 22.
- Tests: unit tests in `nova-resolver`, `nova-typeck`, `nova-std` and
  `nova-codegen-wasm`; `crates/nova-mir/tests/lower_tests.rs`;
  `crates/nova-cli/tests/wasm_tests.rs`, which runs WASM builds under Node;
  differential programs in `tests/wasm/`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`,
approved by the user on 2026-10-14.
- Read it before Task 1: it is the authority this plan argues from.
- Its §13 lists the decisions made while writing it, and its §2 the code as
  it stood.

- **A fault of the environment, not the code:** two worktrees sharing one
  `target/` directory share artifact names, so cargo can take one tree's

## Global Constraints

- **The workspace's minimum Rust stays 1.78,** edition 2021. CI's MSRV job
  runs `cargo check --locked --workspace` with `RUSTFLAGS=-D warnings`. No
  `Option::is_none_or` (1.82), no `std::path::absolute` (1.79).
- **`Cargo.lock` changes twice:** by the 29 lines Task 2 shows, and by the
  one line Task 5 shows. Any other change to it stops the task: ask the
  user.
- **No `unwrap()` outside `#[cfg(test)]` and test files:** `expect("why")`
  or `?`.
- **Tests never touch the internet.** Every CLI test sets `NOVA_HOME` to a
  fresh directory. Every test directory has a fixed name under the system's
  temporary directory, removed and made again at the test's start. Node
  runs only what a test built.
- **A test that needs Node** passes without running when `node` is not on
  `PATH`, and fails instead under `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1`, which CI sets
  (spec §9.1). Run the WASM tests here with `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1`: Node 22
  is installed.
- **`nova-cli`'s tests need two files beside `target/debug/nova`:**
  `libnova_runtime.a`, which every native build links, and the
  `nova-bench-http` binary, which `run_tests` runs. Task 0's workspace
  build puts both there, and no task changes them. A `-p` build does not:
  after a `cargo clean`, run `cargo build --locked --workspace` again
  before a `-p nova-cli` run. Without them, `nova run`, `nova test` and the
  differential tests fail with "could not find the Nova runtime library",
  and `bench_http_server_and_generator_agree` with
  "`CARGO_BIN_EXE_nova-bench-http` is unset".
- **Nothing is published, and no tag is pushed.** Merge by rebase, on the
  user's word only. Never enable auto-merge.
- **This machine is Linux.** Windows and macOS results come from CI, which
  runs all three systems, Node included.

## Conventions

- **Scratch directory:** `$P` is the session's scratch directory, outside
  the repository. Set it in every shell before a command that names
  `$P`.
- **The ledger:** `$P/ledger-40x.md`. Every ruling made while executing goes
  there, with its cost if wrong; so do each task's figures.
- **Commits.** Each task's message is written to `$P/msg-40-<task>.txt`:
  the subject the task names, a blank line, and the session's attribution
  lines. Then `git add -A`; `git status --short` lists only the task's
  files; and `git commit -F "$P/msg-40-<task>.txt"`.
- **The baseline's full output** is `$P/base-40.txt` (Task 0).
- **The patches.** Each task's code is in patch blocks below, each
  marked `<!-- patch NAME -->` and fenced with four backticks. They are the
  prototype's commits, so apply them; do not retype them. From the
  repository's root:

  ```sh
  PLAN=docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations.md   # this file, committed
  block() {
    awk -v name="$1" '
      $0 == "<!-- patch " name " -->" { grab = 1; next }
      grab && /^````diff$/ { inside = 1; next }
      inside && /^````$/ { exit }
      inside { print }
    ' "$PLAN" > "$P/$1.patch"
  }
  patch_block() {
    block "$1" && git apply --check "$P/$1.patch" && git apply "$P/$1.patch"
  }
  ```

  `patch_block t1-tests` applies the block named `t1-tests`; `block`
  only extracts one, as the lockfile's are, to compare. A block that does
  not apply means the tree differs from the plan's: stop, and record why
  in the ledger.
- **Generated files are generated, not patched:** `Cargo.lock` (by cargo),
  the licence files (copied), and each `tests/wasm/*.stdout` (by `nova run`;
  its SHA-256 is given).
- **Each task** is test first where its tests are separate files: the tests'
  patch, their run and the expected failure, then the code's patch, its run
  and the expected pass. Where a task's tests are unit tests in the files it
  changes, its one patch carries both, and Task 10's mutants show they
  bite. Each task then runs its checks (Task 1's Step 5): `cargo fmt --all
  -- --check`, the workspace's clippy with `--all-targets --all-features
  -D warnings` as CI runs it, and `nova fmt --check std examples` when it
  changes `std/`. It ends with one commit. A difference from an `Expected:` is fixed in the
  smallest way and recorded in the ledger as a ruling, with its cost if
  wrong.

## Review Focus

The inputs most likely to bite a user, most likely first. Each has its test
in the task that owns the code:

1. **A `Float`'s text in an exact tie,** where JS and Rust choose
   differently; fixed precision on a tie, where `toFixed` and Rust
   differ; and text read as a `Float` outside the grammar, where `Number`
   accepts what Rust refuses → `float_text_matches_nova_runtime`,
   `float_fixed_matches_nova_runtime` and `float_from_is_rusts_parse`
   (Task 8), and mutants 7, 8 and 14.
2. **A runtime call routed to `std/rt`,** where MIR passes a `Ptr` and the
   function takes an `Int`: a missed conversion makes an invalid module →
   `a_call_into_std_rt_converts_between_ptr_and_int` (Task 4), mutant 3.
3. **A loop with `break` and `continue`,** which the structuring must nest
   right → `loops_with_break_and_continue_are_structured` (Task 2), the
   gate's `break_continue` and `for_loops` (Task 8), and mutant 4.
4. **A program WASM cannot run** (`async fn main`, `extern`) must be refused
   with a named error, not miscompiled → the E0090 and E0091 tests (Tasks 1
   and 3), mutants 1 and 6.
5. **A panic or a trap under Node** must end as natively, with the message
   and the status → `a_panic_exits_134_with_its_message`,
   `a_trap_exits_132` (Task 6), and the gate's five panicking programs.
6. **The release set:** a published crate that depends on an unpublished
   one cannot be packed → `release_manifests.rs` (Task 5), and the package
   gate (Task 10).

## Decisions: where this plan settles what the spec leaves open

Each was a ruling while prototyping; each is open to the user's review.
Decisions 1, 8, 18, 23 and 24 differ from a sentence of the approved
spec, and decision 25 from its names.

1. **`Cargo.lock` changes by 30 lines, not 29.** The 29 are spec §2's
   count, with the packages it names (Task 2). The 30th is `nova-driver`'s list gaining
   `nova-codegen-wasm` (Task 5): spec §8 names that dependency, but §2
   measured `nova-codegen-wasm` alone.
2. **The resolver's enum is `CompileTarget`:** `nova_resolver` already
   exports `index::Target`, the language server's. `nova_driver` re-exports
   it as `Target`, the spec's name.
3. **`std/rt` is `nova_std::RT`, outside `nova_std::ALL`.** nova-doc's tests
   iterate `ALL` and ask each module for docs, an example and a page, which
   an internal, WASM-only module has none of. nova-std's own test counts 18
   files on disk: `ALL`'s and `rt`.
4. **nova-doc lists `Builtin::GLOBAL`** where it listed "every builtin but
   the std-only ones", which would now include the WASM-only ones. The five
   are the same, in the same order.
5. **The memory builtins lower to a new `Stmt::Mem { dst, op: MemOp, args
   }`,** not to `RtFunc` variants: both native backends declare every
   `RtFunc` and tie it to a runtime symbol, which these have none of. Both
   refuse `Stmt::Mem` as an internal error.
6. **`RtFunc::on_wasm()` is the one table of three of spec §4.6's four
   kinds,** exhaustive over all 94 runtime functions: 26 `std/rt`, 11
   host, 57 unavailable. The fourth kind, the nine intrinsics, are
   `Stmt::Mem` (decision 5), not runtime functions.
7. **Routing runs inside mono's worklist** (a lowered function's calls to
   `std/rt` functions become `Call`s, and those functions join the
   worklist), and once more after the async transform, whose state objects
   call `RtFunc::Alloc`. `rt_alloc` is a root, so it is lowered by then.
   E0091 is checked last, over the whole module (the `async fn main` shim
   included), once per runtime function, labelled at `main`. A function
   `std/rt` lacks is E0091 too.
8. **A `Switch` is a chain of comparisons,** where spec §4.2 says a
   `br_table` when dense. Each arm's target is a block the switch alone
   reaches, which the chain inlines; a `br_table` needs every target to be
   a label.
9. **`nova-codegen-wasm` keeps its stub's dependencies,** `nova-diagnostics`
   and `tracing`, though unused, so the lock changes by exactly the
   approved lines. `nova-mir` re-exports `BinOp`, so the backend needs no
   `nova-hir`.
10. **Two scratch `i32` locals per function:** the dispatch loop's `$pc`,
    and an address a statement builds in two steps (a closure's
    environment, an array element's address).
11. **`call_indirect` types are interned before any function is lowered,**
    so a function's lowering only reads the module's layout.
12. **The shim's text is in `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/shim/`**
    (`core.js`, `run.js`, `float.js`, `index.html`). Each host function is
    an `env` entry in `shim.rs`, one line but for `panic`'s four; an
    import with no entry is an error. `float.js` and the seed come only
    with an import that uses them.
13. **`node dist/app.js` relies on Node 22 detecting ES module syntax** in a
    `.js` file with no `package.json`; it is silent on 22.22 here, and CI
    installs Node 22.
14. **`str_hash`'s seed is `splitmix(int_hash_seed() ^ 0x5555…)`,** drawn
    once into state words 16 and 24: a WASM run draws one random value from
    the shim, where the native runtime draws two.
15. **`std/rt`'s string basics come in Task 3,** so routing has something
    to route in its tests; Task 7 adds the rest.
16. **`--target` takes `native` or `wasm`** (`TargetArg`), on `build` and
    `check`. `nova bundle` takes the file and `-o`.
17. **A stack overflow** is V8's `RangeError` whose message names the call
    stack; the shim prints `nova: stack overflow` and exits 134.
18. **`crates/README.md` is unchanged:** it is the published crates' shared
    README and lists no crate, where spec §10 lists it among the records
    to change.
19. **The next release's token keeps `publish-new`,** since the next
    release adds a crate; Task 9 notes it in the runbook.
20. **`flate2` is `nova-cli`'s dev-dependency** for the size check, which
    changes no lockfile line: it is in `nova-cli`'s list already, as a
    build dependency.
21. **The gate is `mod gate` in `wasm_tests.rs`, one test per program.** A
    panicking program is checked as `run_tests.rs` checks it natively, and
    also for ending by its own panic, not by a stack overflow or a trap.
22. **The differential programs** count `Float`s by adding (Nova has no
    `Int` to `Float` conversion) and build strings by interpolation (it
    has no `+` on strings).
23. **The memory builtins are `Builtin::WASM_ONLY`,** where spec §3.3 says
    they "are `Builtin::STD_ONLY`". `STD_ONLY`'s builtins are seeded into
    every target's std; these only into WASM's, which a list of their own
    says.
24. **`str_to_float`'s differential test is a unit test,**
    `float_from_is_rusts_parse` in `shim.rs`, where spec §9.3 puts every
    differential program in `tests/wasm/`. `str_to_float` is std's alone,
    so no Nova program can hand it a text outside JSON's number grammar.
    The test runs the shim's own `floatFrom` under Node over every case of
    spec §6's grammar, valid and not, and compares bits with Rust's
    `str::parse::<f64>().unwrap_or(0.0)`.
25. **Names the spec gives otherwise:**
    - `std/rt`'s functions are named `rt_` and the runtime symbol without
      `nova_rt_` (`rt_alloc`, `rt_str_alloc`), where spec §5.2 and §5.3
      say `alloc` and `str_alloc`;
    - `mem_load8` and `mem_store8` lower to `i64.load8_u` and
      `i64.store8`, since their values are `Int`s, where spec §5.1 names
      the `i32` forms, which would need a conversion beside each;
    - test directories are under `std::env::temp_dir()`, as every CLI
      test in the repository has them, where spec §9.1 says the test
      target's temporary directory.

## Contracts between tasks

- **Task 1** gives:
  - `nova_resolver::{CompileTarget, WASM_STD_MODULES, resolve_program_for}`
    and `Builtin::WASM_ONLY`;
  - `nova_driver::{Target, check_program_for, check_program_counted_for}`;
  - `nova_mir::{MemOp, Stmt::Mem}`;
  - `nova_std::RT`, and `nova check --target <native|wasm>`.
- **Task 2** gives `nova_mir::{WasmRuntime, BinOp}`, `RtFunc::on_wasm()`,
  and `nova_codegen_wasm::{compile_module, Output, DATA_BASE,
  MEMORY_PAGES, MEMORY_MAX_PAGES}`. `Output { wasm, imports }`: `imports`
  are the `env` field names, in import order.
- **Task 3** gives `nova_mir::{RuntimeImpls, Options, lower_module_with}`
  and `Module::runtime_alloc`. A `std/rt` function is named `rt_` and the
  runtime symbol without its `nova_rt_` prefix (`rt_str_concat`), and takes
  and returns `Int` addresses where the native function takes pointers.
- **Task 4:** `compile_module` lowers every statement, and exports
  `nova_alloc` when `runtime_alloc` is set.
- **Task 6** gives `nova_codegen_wasm::{shim, INDEX_HTML}` (`shim(imports)
  -> Result<String>`), `nova_driver::build_program_wasm(program, out_dir)`,
  `nova build --target wasm [-o DIR]` and `nova bundle [FILE] [-o DIR]`.
  - `app.js` exports `default async function init(options)`, which takes
    `options.wasm` (the module's bytes) or loads `app.wasm` beside itself.
  - Run as `node app.js`, it calls `init()` itself.
  - A panic writes `nova: panic: <msg>` and exits 134; a trap writes
    `nova: trap: <message>` and exits 132.
- **Task 8:** the shim provides all 11 host functions.

### Task 0: The baseline

The branch is at this plan's commit, whose parent is `62aed1d`: the
approved spec, and the gate's five panicking programs. The plan's commit
adds this file alone, so the patches apply to it as to `62aed1d`.

- [ ] **Step 1: The tree.**
  - `git status --short` prints nothing, on
    `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`.
  - `git diff --stat 62aed1d HEAD` lists only
    `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations.md`.
  - `BASE=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)`: the plan's commit, recorded in
    the ledger. Task 10's review reads from it.
  - `df -h /` shows at least 15G free; if not, run `cargo clean` first.
- [ ] **Step 2: Node.** `node --version` prints `v22.` and more. From
  Task 6, CI installs Node 22.
- [ ] **Step 3: The suite.**

  ```sh
  cargo build --locked --workspace
  CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast > "$P/base-40.txt" 2>&1
  grep '^test result' "$P/base-40.txt" | awk '{p += $4; f += $6; i += $8} END {print p, f, i}'
  grep ' FAILED$' "$P/base-40.txt"
  ```

  Expected: `2022 1 10`, and one failure, `test
  net::tests::an_ipv6_connect_parks_on_its_first_poll_and_then_establishes
  ... FAILED`. It needs IPv6, which this machine lacks, as in every
  earlier phase.
- [ ] **Step 4: The ledger.** Start `$P/ledger-40x.md`, headed with the
  base commit and the baseline's figures.

### Task 1: The target, WASM's std, the memory builtins, E0090, `check --target`

Spec §3, §5.1 and §5.2; decisions 2 to 5.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/nova-cli/tests/wasm_tests.rs`, `std/rt/lib.nova`
- Modify:
  - `crates/nova-resolver/src/lib.rs`
  - `crates/nova-typeck/src/check.rs`
  - `crates/nova-mir/src/{lib,lower,async_lower}.rs`
  - `crates/nova-codegen-{cranelift,llvm}/src/lib.rs`
  - `crates/nova-doc/src/model.rs`
  - `crates/nova-driver/src/lib.rs`
  - `crates/nova-cli/src/cmd/run.rs`
  - `std/lib.rs`

- [ ] **Step 1: The tests.** `patch_block t1-tests` creates
  `wasm_tests.rs`. It holds the helpers every later task's tests use:
  - `nova()`;
  - `fresh(name)`, a fixed directory under the system's temporary
    directory, made again;
  - `write`;
  - `nova_in(dir, args)`, with `NOVA_HOME` beside the directory,
    `NO_COLOR=1` and `RUST_BACKTRACE=0`;
  - `stdout`, `stderr`.

  And four tests of `nova check --target wasm`:
  - `check_for_wasm_accepts_a_program`: `let n = 6 * 7`, printed.
  - `check_for_wasm_refuses_an_extern_block`: E0090, and accepted
    natively.
  - `check_for_wasm_has_no_native_only_module`: `args()` (std/process) is
    E0001 on WASM.
  - `a_user_module_cannot_call_a_memory_builtin`: `mem_load64` is E0001
    in a user's module, even on WASM.
- [ ] **Step 2: Run, and see them fail.**
  `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test -p nova-cli --test wasm_tests`.
  Expected: `0 passed; 4 failed`, each stderr `error: unexpected argument
  '--target' found`.
- [ ] **Step 3: The code.** `patch_block t1-code`:
  - **`nova-resolver`:**
    - `CompileTarget { Native, Wasm }`, with `std_modules()` and `name()`;
    - `WASM_STD_MODULES`: core, bytes, collections, strings, fmt, json,
      and `("$std.rt", nova_std::RT)`;
    - the nine `Builtin`s `mem_load8` … `heap_base`, as
      `Builtin::WASM_ONLY`, seeded only into a WASM build's std modules;
    - `resolve_program_for(target, …)`, which `resolve_program` calls
      with `Native`;
    - E0090, "extern functions are not available on target wasm", at the
      `extern` block;
    - three unit tests.
  - **`nova-std`:** `RT`, outside `ALL`; its test counts 18 files.
    `std/rt/lib.nova` holds `rt_alloc`, the bump allocator: state word 8
    is the next free address, it grows the memory by whole pages, and it
    panics "out of memory".
  - **`nova-typeck`:** the nine signatures, and a unit test that a user's
    module cannot call them.
  - **`nova-mir`:** `MemOp` and `Stmt::Mem { dst, op, args }`, which the
    nine builtins lower to; the async transform's use and def arms.
  - **The native backends:** both refuse `Stmt::Mem` as an internal
    error.
  - **`nova-doc`:** lists `Builtin::GLOBAL` (decision 4).
  - **`nova-driver`:** `Target`, `check_program_for`,
    `check_program_counted_for`; `FrontendContext` carries the target and
    resolves against its std.
  - **`nova-cli`:** `TargetArg` and `check --target <native|wasm>`.
- [ ] **Step 4: Run.**
  - `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test -p nova-cli --test wasm_tests`.
    Expected: `4 passed`.
  - `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p nova-resolver -p nova-typeck -p
    nova-std -p nova-mir -p nova-doc -p nova-driver -p
    nova-codegen-cranelift -p nova-codegen-llvm -p nova-cli`, then sum
    its `test result` lines as Task 0 does. Expected: `1315 0 2`.
- [ ] **Step 5: Checks.**
  - `cargo fmt --all -- --check` prints nothing.
  - `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
    ends with `Finished`, and no warning or error.
  - `cargo run --locked -q -p nova-cli -- fmt --check std examples`
    prints nothing.
- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**
  `wasm: the target, WASM's std, the memory builtins, E0090, check --target`.

**Patch `t1-tests`:**

<!-- patch t1-tests -->

````diff
diff --git a/crates/nova-cli/tests/wasm_tests.rs b/crates/nova-cli/tests/wasm_tests.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..a518f6c
--- /dev/null
+++ b/crates/nova-cli/tests/wasm_tests.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,124 @@
+//! The WASM target, end to end (spec
+//! `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`).
+
+use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
+
+use assert_cmd::assert::Assert;
+use assert_cmd::Command;
+
+fn nova() -> Command {
+    Command::cargo_bin("nova").expect("nova binary builds")
+}
+
+/// A fresh, empty directory with a fixed name, so each run replaces the
+/// last.
+fn fresh(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
+    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("nova-wasm-test-{name}"));
+    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
+    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect("create the test directory");
+    dir
+}
+
+fn write(dir: &Path, files: &[(&str, &str)]) {
+    for (path, text) in files {
+        let path = dir.join(path);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().expect("a parent")).expect("create a directory");
+        std::fs::write(path, text).expect("write a file");
+    }
+}
+
+/// `nova ARGS` in `dir`, with a fresh `NOVA_HOME` beside it, no colour and
+/// no backtrace.
+fn nova_in(dir: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> Assert {
+    let home = dir.with_file_name(format!(
+        "{}-home",
+        dir.file_name().expect("a name").to_string_lossy()
+    ));
+    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&home);
+    nova()
+        .current_dir(dir)
+        .env("NOVA_HOME", &home)
+        .env("NO_COLOR", "1")
+        .env("RUST_BACKTRACE", "0")
+        .args(args)
+        .assert()
+}
+
+fn stdout(a: &Assert) -> String {
+    String::from_utf8_lossy(&a.get_output().stdout).replace("\r\n", "\n")
+}
+
+fn stderr(a: &Assert) -> String {
+    String::from_utf8_lossy(&a.get_output().stderr).replace("\r\n", "\n")
+}
+
+/// A program WASM can build (spec §3.1).
+#[test]
+fn check_for_wasm_accepts_a_program() {
+    let dir = fresh("check-ok");
+    write(
+        &dir,
+        &[(
+            "main.nova",
+            "fn main() {\n    let n = 6 * 7\n    println(\"${n}\")\n}\n",
+        )],
+    );
+    let a = nova_in(&dir, &["check", "--target", "wasm", "main.nova"]).success();
+    assert_eq!(stdout(&a), "ok: main.nova\n");
+}
+
+/// `extern` is E0090 on WASM, and fine natively (spec §3.3).
+#[test]
+fn check_for_wasm_refuses_an_extern_block() {
+    let dir = fresh("check-extern");
+    write(
+        &dir,
+        &[(
+            "main.nova",
+            "extern \"C\" {\n    fn abs(x: Int) -> Int\n}\nfn main() { println(\"${abs(-3)}\") }\n",
+        )],
+    );
+    let a = nova_in(&dir, &["check", "--target", "wasm", "main.nova"]).failure();
+    assert!(
+        stderr(&a).contains("error[E0090]: extern functions are not available on target wasm"),
+        "{}",
+        stderr(&a)
+    );
+    nova_in(&dir, &["check", "--target", "native", "main.nova"]).success();
+    nova_in(&dir, &["check", "main.nova"]).success();
+}
+
+/// A native-only module is not compiled for WASM, so its names do not
+/// resolve (spec §3.2).
+#[test]
+fn check_for_wasm_has_no_native_only_module() {
+    let dir = fresh("check-process");
+    write(
+        &dir,
+        &[("main.nova", "fn main() { println(\"${args().len()}\") }\n")],
+    );
+    let a = nova_in(&dir, &["check", "--target", "wasm", "main.nova"]).failure();
+    assert!(
+        stderr(&a).contains("error[E0001]: cannot find function `args` in this scope"),
+        "{}",
+        stderr(&a)
+    );
+    nova_in(&dir, &["check", "main.nova"]).success();
+}
+
+/// The memory builtins are `std/rt`'s: a user module cannot call one, even
+/// on WASM (spec §3.3).
+#[test]
+fn a_user_module_cannot_call_a_memory_builtin() {
+    let dir = fresh("check-mem");
+    write(
+        &dir,
+        &[("main.nova", "fn main() { println(\"${mem_load64(8)}\") }\n")],
+    );
+    let a = nova_in(&dir, &["check", "--target", "wasm", "main.nova"]).failure();
+    assert!(
+        stderr(&a).contains("error[E0001]: cannot find function `mem_load64` in this scope"),
+        "{}",
+        stderr(&a)
+    );
+}
````

+
+/// A WASM memory access, the lowering of one of `std/rt`'s memory builtins
+/// (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`
+/// §5.1). Addresses and values are `I64`; the WASM backend wraps an address
+/// to 32 bits. Only a WASM build's std calls the builtins, so no native
+/// backend emits these: both refuse one as an internal error.
+#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum MemOp {
+    /// `(addr) -> byte`, zero-extended.
+    Load8,
+    /// `(addr, v)`: `v`'s low byte.
+    Store8,
+    /// `(addr) -> word`.
+    Load64,
+    /// `(addr, v)`.
+    Store64,
+    /// `(dst, src, n)`, which may overlap.
+    Copy,
+    /// `(dst, byte, n)`.
+    Fill,
+    /// `() -> pages`.
+    Pages,
+    /// `(pages) -> old pages`, or -1.
+    Grow,
+    /// `() -> the heap's first address`.
+    HeapBase,
 }
 
 /// Block terminators.

+    MemFill,

+    MemPages,

+    MemGrow,

+++ b/std/rt/lib.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,34 @@
+// Nova standard library — the runtime of a WASM build.
+//
+// Compiled only when the target is WASM (spec
+// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md §5).
+// Where a native build calls a `nova-runtime` function, MIR calls the
+// function here of the same name with an `rt_` prefix, and each is a port of
+// that one, with the same results. None is `pub`, so no program sees them.
+//
+// A runtime value is an `Int` address. A `String` or `Bytes` value is a
+// 16-byte header: its length at offset 0, and at offset 8 the address of its
+// bytes. An array is `{len, elems...}`, element `i` at offset `8 + 8*i`.
+// Memory is read and written through the `mem_*` builtins, which only this
+// target's std sees.
+
+// The runtime's state words, at fixed addresses below the data segment
+// (spec §4.1): Nova has no mutable globals.
+
+// The next free address, or 0 before the first allocation.
+const HEAP_NEXT: Int = 8
+
+// Allocate `size` bytes, zeroed, 8-aligned (spec §5.2). The heap only grows:
+// the collector is 4.1's.
+fn rt_alloc(size: Int) -> Int {
+    let mut n = size
+    if n < 8 { n = 8 }
+    n = (n + 7) & -8
+    let mut at = mem_load64(HEAP_NEXT)
+    if at == 0 { at = heap_base() }
+    let end = at + n
+    let have = mem_pages() * 65536
+    if end > have { if mem_grow((end - have + 65535) / 65536) < 0 { panic("out of memory") } }
+    mem_store64(HEAP_NEXT, end)
+    at
+}
````

### Task 2: The backend's core

Spec §4.1 to §4.3, §4.6 and §4.7; decisions 5, 6, 8 and 9, and decision
10's `$pc`. The tests are
unit tests in the files this task creates, so its one patch carries both.
Task 10's mutant 4 shows that they bite.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/structure.rs`
- Modify:
  - `Cargo.toml`, `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/Cargo.toml`
  - `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs`, the stub
  - `crates/nova-mir/src/lib.rs`
- Generated: `Cargo.lock`

- [ ] **Step 1: The code.** `patch_block t2-code`:
  - **The workspace's dependencies:** `wasm-encoder = "=0.243.0"`; and
    `wasmparser`, the same version, without default features but with
    `std`, `validate`, `features` and `simd`, as the backend's
    dev-dependency.
  - **`nova-mir`:**
    - `WasmRuntime { Nova(name), Host(name), Unavailable }`;
    - `RtFunc::on_wasm()`, over all 94 runtime functions: 26 `std/rt`,
      11 host, 57 unavailable;
    - `BinOp`, re-exported.
  - **`nova-codegen-wasm`, `lib.rs`:**
    - `compile_module(mir) -> Result<Output { wasm, imports }>`;
    - the constants `DATA_BASE` (64), `MEMORY_PAGES` (256) and
      `MEMORY_MAX_PAGES` (16384).
    - It builds one module:
      - the value mapping: `I64` → `i64`, `F64` → `f64`, `I8` and `Ptr`
        → `i32`, `Unit` → nothing;
      - each host function, imported from `env` by name;
      - the memory, exported as `memory`, and `main`, exported.
    - It lowers the scalar statements, calls, `Stmt::Mem` (in line) and
      the terminators.
    - It refuses, as internal errors:
      - a heap statement, "not lowered yet", until Task 4;
      - a runtime function that is not the shim's, until Task 3 routes
        them;
      - `Float` `%`, as both native backends do.
  - **`structure.rs`:**
    - reverse postorder by an explicit stack;
    - Cooper, Harvey and Kennedy's dominators;
    - merge nodes, loop headers, and whether the graph is reducible.

    `lib.rs` nests the dominator tree as Ramsey's "Beyond Relooper" does.
    An irreducible graph becomes a dispatch loop on a scratch `$pc`. A
    `Switch` becomes a chain of comparisons (decision 8).
  - **Tests:**
    - nine in `lib.rs`, which validate each module they build with
      `wasmparser`, among them
      `loops_with_break_and_continue_are_structured`;
    - five in `structure.rs`.

````diff

index fd903d0..e6bbb24 100644
--- a/Cargo.toml
+++ b/Cargo.toml
@@ -64,6 +64,12 @@ cranelift-jit = "0.105"
 cranelift-native = "0.105"
 cranelift-object = "0.105"
 cc = "1"
+# Phase 4's WASM backend (spec
+# docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md §8):
+# 0.243.0 is the last release of each that declares a minimum Rust under
+# the workspace's 1.78. `wasmparser` only validates what tests build.
+wasm-encoder = "=0.243.0"
+wasmparser = { version = "=0.243.0", default-features = false, features = ["std", "validate", "features", "simd"] }
 
 # Tooling
 clap = { version = "4", features = ["derive"] }
diff --git a/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/Cargo.toml b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/Cargo.toml
index 005e704..31b6c4f 100644
--- a/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/Cargo.toml
+++ b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/Cargo.toml
@@ -23,3 +23,7 @@ nova-diagnostics = { workspace = true }
 nova-mir = { workspace = true }
 anyhow = { workspace = true }
 tracing = { workspace = true }
+wasm-encoder = { workspace = true }
+
+[dev-dependencies]
+wasmparser = { workspace = true }
diff --git a/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs
index 53d33de..693e76f 100644
--- a/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs
+++ b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs
@@ -1 +1,1148 @@
-//! See ARCHITECTURE.md for crate purpose and pipeline context.
+//! The WASM backend: one MIR module to one WASM module (spec
+//! `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`
+//! §4). See ARCHITECTURE.md for the pipeline.
+//!
+//! Values map as `I64` → `i64`, `F64` → `f64`, `I8` → `i32` (0 or 1),
+//! `Ptr` → `i32`, and `Unit` to no value; each non-`Unit` temp is a local.
+//! Blocks are structured by [`structure`]'s analysis, or, for an
+//! irreducible graph, run by a dispatch loop. Runtime functions are of three
+//! kinds on WASM ([`nova_mir::WasmRuntime`]): `std/rt`'s, which MIR already
+//! calls directly; the shim's, imported from `env`; and none.
+
+mod structure;
+
+use std::borrow::Cow;
+use std::collections::HashMap;
+
+use anyhow::{anyhow, bail, Result};
+use nova_mir::{
+    BinOp, Function as MirFn, MemOp, MirTy, Module, OperandClass, RtFunc, Stmt, Temp, Terminator,
+    WasmRuntime,
+};
+use wasm_encoder::{
+    BlockType, CodeSection, EntityType, ExportKind, ExportSection, Function, FunctionSection,
+    ImportSection, Instruction as I, MemArg, MemorySection, MemoryType, TypeSection, ValType,
+};
+
+use structure::{Cfg, UNREACHED};
+
+/// Memory's first size, in 64 KiB pages: 16 MB (`30-FRONTEND.md` §10).
+pub const MEMORY_PAGES: u64 = 256;
+/// Memory's largest size, in pages: 1 GB.
+pub const MEMORY_MAX_PAGES: u64 = 16384;
+/// Where the data segment starts (spec §4.1): `0..8` is never an object,
+/// and `8..64` holds `std/rt`'s state words.
+pub const DATA_BASE: u32 = 64;
+
+/// What [`compile_module`] makes.
+#[derive(Debug)]
+pub struct Output {
+    /// The module's bytes.
+    pub wasm: Vec<u8>,
+    /// The shim's functions it imports from `env`, by field name, in the
+    /// order of the module's imports.
+    pub imports: Vec<&'static str>,
+}
+
+/// The WASM value of a MIR class, or none for `Unit`.
+fn val(ty: MirTy) -> Option<ValType> {
+    match ty {
+        MirTy::I64 => Some(ValType::I64),
+        MirTy::F64 => Some(ValType::F64),
+        MirTy::I8 | MirTy::Ptr => Some(ValType::I32),
+        MirTy::Unit => None,
+    }
+}
+
+fn vals(tys: &[MirTy]) -> Vec<ValType> {
+    tys.iter().filter_map(|t| val(*t)).collect()
+}
+
+/// A deduplicating type section.
+#[derive(Default)]
+struct Types {
+    section: TypeSection,
+    index: HashMap<(Vec<ValType>, Vec<ValType>), u32>,
+}
+
+impl Types {
+    fn of(&mut self, params: Vec<ValType>, results: Vec<ValType>) -> u32 {
+        let key = (params, results);
+        if let Some(&i) = self.index.get(&key) {
+            return i;
+        }
+        let i = self.index.len() as u32;
+        self.section
+            .ty()
+            .function(key.0.iter().copied(), key.1.iter().copied());
+        self.index.insert(key, i);
+        i
+    }
+}
+
+/// A function's ABI parameters: the environment pointer when it takes one,
+/// then its parameters, as MIR's temps hold them.
+fn abi_params(f: &MirFn) -> &[MirTy] {
+    let n = f.params as usize + usize::from(f.takes_env);
+    &f.temps[..n]
+}
+
+/// What the module knows while lowering each function.
+struct Layout {
+    /// Each function's index, by name: imports first, then MIR's functions.
+    funcs: HashMap<String, u32>,
+    /// Each MIR function's return class, by name.
+    rets: HashMap<String, MirTy>,
+    /// Each MIR function's ABI parameter classes, by name.
+    params: HashMap<String, Vec<MirTy>>,
+    /// Each host function's index, by `RtFunc`.
+    hosts: HashMap<RtFunc, u32>,
+    /// The heap's first address ([`MemOp::HeapBase`]).
+    heap_base: i64,
+}
+
+/// Lower `mir` to a WASM module. `main` is exported, with the memory.
+pub fn compile_module(mir: &Module) -> Result<Output> {
+    let mut types = Types::default();
+    let mut imports = ImportSection::new();
+    let mut layout = Layout {
+        funcs: HashMap::new(),
+        rets: HashMap::new(),
+        params: HashMap::new(),
+        hosts: HashMap::new(),
+        heap_base: i64::from(DATA_BASE),
+    };
+
+    // The host functions the program calls, in first-use order.
+    let mut host_order: Vec<RtFunc> = Vec::new();
+    for f in &mir.functions {
+        for b in &f.blocks {
+            for s in &b.stmts {
+                if let Stmt::CallRuntime { func, .. } = s {
+                    match func.on_wasm() {
+                        WasmRuntime::Host(_) => {
+                            if !host_order.contains(func) {
+                                host_order.push(*func);
+                            }
+                        }
+                        WasmRuntime::Nova(name) => bail!(
+                            "`{}` reached the WASM backend: MIR must call `std/rt`'s `{name}`",
+                            func.symbol()
+                        ),
+                        WasmRuntime::Unavailable => bail!(
+                            "`{}` reached the WASM backend, which has no such function",
+                            func.symbol()
+                        ),
+                    }
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    let mut import_names = Vec::new();
+    for (i, func) in host_order.iter().enumerate() {
+        let WasmRuntime::Host(name) = func.on_wasm() else {
+            unreachable!("only host functions are collected");
+        };
+        let (params, ret) = func.signature();
+        let ty = types.of(vals(&params), val(ret).into_iter().collect());
+        imports.import("env", name, EntityType::Function(ty));
+        layout.hosts.insert(*func, i as u32);
+        import_names.push(name);
+    }
+    let first = host_order.len() as u32;
+    for (i, f) in mir.functions.iter().enumerate() {
+        layout.funcs.insert(f.name.clone(), first + i as u32);
+        layout.rets.insert(f.name.clone(), f.ret);
+        layout.params.insert(f.name.clone(), abi_params(f).to_vec());
+    }
+
+    let mut functions = FunctionSection::new();
+    let mut code = CodeSection::new();
+    for f in &mir.functions {
+        let ty = types.of(vals(abi_params(f)), val(f.ret).into_iter().collect());
+        functions.function(ty);
+        let body = FnLower::new(f, &layout)
+            .lower()
+            .map_err(|e| anyhow!("compiling `{}`: {e}", f.name))?;
+        code.function(&body);
+    }
+
+    let mut module = wasm_encoder::Module::new();
+    module.section(&types.section);
+    module.section(&imports);
+    module.section(&functions);
+    let mut memories = MemorySection::new();
+    memories.memory(MemoryType {
+        minimum: MEMORY_PAGES,
+        maximum: Some(MEMORY_MAX_PAGES),
+        memory64: false,
+        shared: false,
+        page_size_log2: None,
+    });
+    module.section(&memories);
+    let mut exports = ExportSection::new();
+    let main = layout
+        .funcs
+        .get("main")
+        .copied()
+        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("the module has no `main`"))?;
+    exports.export("main", ExportKind::Func, main);
+    exports.export("memory", ExportKind::Memory, 0);
+    module.section(&exports);
+    module.section(&code);
+    Ok(Output {
+        wasm: module.finish(),
+        imports: import_names,
+    })
+}
+
+/// A label the code being emitted is nested in.
+#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
+enum Ctx {
+    /// The `loop` whose header is this block: `br` to it continues.
+    Loop(usize),
+    /// The `block` this block follows: `br` to it goes there.
+    Block(usize),
+    /// An `if`, which no branch targets.
+    If,
+}
+
+/// One function's lowering.
+struct FnLower<'a> {
+    f: &'a MirFn,
+    layout: &'a Layout,
+    /// Each temp's local, or none for `Unit`.
+    locals: Vec<Option<u32>>,
+    /// The dispatch loop's block number, when the graph is irreducible.
+    pc: u32,
+    decls: Vec<(u32, ValType)>,
+    out: Vec<I<'static>>,
+    ctx: Vec<Ctx>,
+    cfg: Cfg,
+}
+
+fn mem(offset: u64, align: u32) -> MemArg {
+    MemArg {
+        offset,
+        align,
+        memory_index: 0,
+    }
+}
+
+impl<'a> FnLower<'a> {
+    fn new(f: &'a MirFn, layout: &'a Layout) -> Self {
+        let nparams = abi_params(f).len();
+        let mut locals = vec![None; f.temps.len()];
+        let mut next = 0u32;
+        for (t, ty) in f.temps.iter().enumerate().take(nparams) {
+            if val(*ty).is_some() {
+                locals[t] = Some(next);
+                next += 1;
+            }
+        }
+        let mut decls = Vec::new();
+        for (t, ty) in f.temps.iter().enumerate().skip(nparams) {
+            if let Some(v) = val(*ty) {
+                locals[t] = Some(next);
+                next += 1;
+                decls.push((1, v));
+            }
+        }
+        let pc = next;
+        decls.push((1, ValType::I32));
+        FnLower {
+            f,
+            layout,
+            locals,
+            pc,
+            decls,
+            out: Vec::new(),
+            ctx: Vec::new(),
+            cfg: structure::analyze(&f.blocks),
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn lower(mut self) -> Result<Function> {
+        if self.f.blocks.is_empty() {
+            bail!("a function with no blocks");
+        }
+        if self.cfg.reducible {
+            self.tree(0)?;
+        } else {
+            self.dispatch()?;
+        }
+        // Every path above ends in `br`, `return` or `unreachable`, but
+        // after an `end` WASM's validator sees the code as reachable again.
+        self.out.push(I::Unreachable);
+        let mut w = Function::new(self.decls.iter().copied());
+        for ins in &self.out {
+            w.instruction(ins);
+        }
+        w.instruction(&I::End);
+        Ok(w)
+    }
+
+    // --- structuring (Ramsey, "Beyond Relooper") -------------------------
+
+    /// The code for `x` and everything it dominates.
+    fn tree(&mut self, x: usize) -> Result<()> {
+        // Merge children, the latest in reverse postorder first: it is the
+        // outermost `block`, so its code comes last.
+        let mut merges: Vec<usize> = self.cfg.children[x]
+            .iter()
+            .copied()
+            .filter(|&c| self.cfg.merge[c])
+            .collect();
+        merges.reverse();
+        if self.cfg.loop_header[x] {
+            self.out.push(I::Loop(BlockType::Empty));
+            self.ctx.push(Ctx::Loop(x));
+            self.within(x, &merges)?;
+            self.ctx.pop();
+            self.out.push(I::End);
+            Ok(())
+        } else {
+            self.within(x, &merges)
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn within(&mut self, x: usize, merges: &[usize]) -> Result<()> {
+        match merges.split_first() {
+            None => self.body(x),
+            Some((&y, rest)) => {
+                self.out.push(I::Block(BlockType::Empty));
+                self.ctx.push(Ctx::Block(y));
+                self.within(x, rest)?;
+                self.ctx.pop();
+                self.out.push(I::End);
+                self.tree(y)
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Go from `from` to `to`: continue a loop, leave to a merge node, or,
+    /// for a block `from` alone reaches, its code in place.
+    fn branch(&mut self, from: usize, to: usize) -> Result<()> {
+        if self.cfg.rpo_index[to] <= self.cfg.rpo_index[from] {
+            self.br(Ctx::Loop(to))
+        } else if self.cfg.merge[to] {
+            self.br(Ctx::Block(to))
+        } else {
+            self.tree(to)
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn br(&mut self, target: Ctx) -> Result<()> {
+        let at = self
+            .ctx
+            .iter()
+            .rposition(|c| *c == target)
+            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("no enclosing label for {target:?}"))?;
+        self.out.push(I::Br((self.ctx.len() - 1 - at) as u32));
+        Ok(())
+    }
+
+    /// Block `x`'s statements, then its terminator.
+    fn body(&mut self, x: usize) -> Result<()> {
+        let f = self.f;
+        for s in &f.blocks[x].stmts {
+            self.stmt(s)?;
+        }
+        match &f.blocks[x].term {
+            Terminator::Goto(b) => self.branch(x, b.0 as usize),
+            Terminator::Branch { cond, then_, else_ } => {
+                self.get(*cond);
+                self.out.push(I::If(BlockType::Empty));
+                self.ctx.push(Ctx::If);
+                self.branch(x, then_.0 as usize)?;
+                self.out.push(I::Else);
+                self.branch(x, else_.0 as usize)?;
+                self.ctx.pop();
+                self.out.push(I::End);
+                Ok(())
+            }
+            Terminator::Switch {
+                disc,
+                arms,
+                default,
+            } => {
+                for (v, b) in arms {
+                    self.disc_eq(*disc, *v);
+                    self.out.push(I::If(BlockType::Empty));
+                    self.ctx.push(Ctx::If);
+                    self.branch(x, b.0 as usize)?;
+                    self.ctx.pop();
+                    self.out.push(I::End);
+                }
+                self.branch(x, default.0 as usize)
+            }
+            Terminator::Return(t) => self.ret(*t),
+            Terminator::Trap => {
+                self.out.push(I::Unreachable);
+                Ok(())
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// `disc == v`, as an `i32` condition.
+    fn disc_eq(&mut self, disc: Temp, v: i64) {
+        self.get(disc);
+        if self.ty(disc) == MirTy::I64 {
+            self.out.push(I::I64Const(v));
+            self.out.push(I::I64Eq);
+        } else {
+            self.out.push(I::I32Const(v as i32));
+            self.out.push(I::I32Eq);
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn ret(&mut self, t: Option<Temp>) -> Result<()> {
+        if val(self.f.ret).is_some() {
+            match t {
+                Some(t) if self.locals[t.0 as usize].is_some() => self.get(t),
+                _ => bail!("a `return` without the value the function returns"),
+            }
+        }
+        self.out.push(I::Return);
+        Ok(())
+    }
+
+    // --- the dispatch loop, for an irreducible graph ----------------------
+
+    /// Every block in one `loop`, chosen by `$pc` through a `br_table`.
+    fn dispatch(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
+        let f = self.f;
+        let n = f.blocks.len() as u32;
+        self.out.push(I::Loop(BlockType::Empty));
+        for _ in 0..n {
+            self.out.push(I::Block(BlockType::Empty));
+        }
+        self.out.push(I::LocalGet(self.pc));
+        self.out
+            .push(I::BrTable(Cow::Owned((0..n).collect()), n - 1));
+        for (bi, block) in f.blocks.iter().enumerate() {
+            self.out.push(I::End);
+            // From block `bi`'s code, the loop is this many labels out.
+            let to_loop = n - 1 - bi as u32;
+            if self.cfg.rpo_index[bi] == UNREACHED {
+                self.out.push(I::Unreachable);
+                continue;
+            }
+            for s in &block.stmts {
+                self.stmt(s)?;
+            }
+            let goto = |out: &mut Vec<I<'static>>, pc: u32, b: u32, depth: u32| {
+                out.push(I::I32Const(b as i32));
+                out.push(I::LocalSet(pc));
+                out.push(I::Br(depth));
+            };
+            match &block.term {
+                Terminator::Goto(b) => goto(&mut self.out, self.pc, b.0, to_loop),
+                Terminator::Branch { cond, then_, else_ } => {
+                    self.out.push(I::I32Const(then_.0 as i32));
+                    self.out.push(I::I32Const(else_.0 as i32));
+                    self.get(*cond);
+                    self.out.push(I::Select);
+                    self.out.push(I::LocalSet(self.pc));
+                    self.out.push(I::Br(to_loop));
+                }
+                Terminator::Switch {
+                    disc,
+                    arms,
+                    default,
+                } => {
+                    for (v, b) in arms {
+                        self.disc_eq(*disc, *v);
+                        self.out.push(I::If(BlockType::Empty));
+                        goto(&mut self.out, self.pc, b.0, to_loop + 1);
+                        self.out.push(I::End);
+                    }
+                    goto(&mut self.out, self.pc, default.0, to_loop);
+                }
+                Terminator::Return(t) => self.ret(*t)?,
+                Terminator::Trap => self.out.push(I::Unreachable),
+            }
+        }
+        self.out.push(I::End);
+        Ok(())
+    }
+
+    // --- statements -------------------------------------------------------
+
+    fn ty(&self, t: Temp) -> MirTy {
+        self.f.temps[t.0 as usize]
+    }
+
+    fn get(&mut self, t: Temp) {
+        if let Some(i) = self.locals[t.0 as usize] {
+            self.out.push(I::LocalGet(i));
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn set(&mut self, t: Temp) {
+        if let Some(i) = self.locals[t.0 as usize] {
+            self.out.push(I::LocalSet(i));
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Store a call's result in `dst`, or drop it.
+    fn result(&mut self, dst: Option<Temp>, ret: MirTy) {
+        if val(ret).is_none() {
+            return;
+        }
+        match dst {
+            Some(d) if self.locals[d.0 as usize].is_some() => self.set(d),
+            _ => self.out.push(I::Drop),
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// An `i64` address on the stack, as WASM's `i32`.
+    fn wrap(&mut self, t: Temp) {
+        self.get(t);
+        self.out.push(I::I32WrapI64);
+    }
+
+    fn stmt(&mut self, s: &Stmt) -> Result<()> {
+        match s {
+            Stmt::ConstInt(t, v) => {
+                self.out.push(I::I64Const(*v));
+                self.set(*t);
+            }
+            Stmt::ConstFloat(t, v) => {
+                self.out.push(I::F64Const((*v).into()));
+                self.set(*t);
+            }
+            Stmt::ConstBool(t, v) => {
+                self.out.push(I::I32Const(i32::from(*v)));
+                self.set(*t);
+            }
+            Stmt::ConstUnit(_) => {}
+            Stmt::Copy { dst, src } => {
+                if self.locals[dst.0 as usize].is_some() {
+                    self.get(*src);
+                    self.set(*dst);
+                }
+            }
+            Stmt::Bin {
+                dst,
+                op,
+                class,
+                lhs,
+                rhs,
+            } => {
+                self.get(*lhs);
+                self.get(*rhs);
+                self.out.push(bin(*op, *class)?);
+                self.set(*dst);
+            }
+            Stmt::Neg { dst, class, src } => {
+                if *class == OperandClass::Float {
+                    self.get(*src);
+                    self.out.push(I::F64Neg);
+                } else {
+                    self.out.push(I::I64Const(0));
+                    self.get(*src);
+                    self.out.push(I::I64Sub);
+                }
+                self.set(*dst);
+            }
+            Stmt::Not { dst, src } => {
+                self.get(*src);
+                self.out.push(I::I32Eqz);
+                self.set(*dst);
+            }
+            Stmt::BitNot { dst, src } => {
+                self.get(*src);
+                self.out.push(I::I64Const(-1));
+                self.out.push(I::I64Xor);
+                self.set(*dst);
+            }
+            Stmt::Call { dst, callee, args } => {
+                let layout = self.layout;
+                let index = *layout
+                    .funcs
+                    .get(callee)
+                    .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("a call to an undeclared `{callee}`"))?;
+                for a in args {
+                    self.get(*a);
+                }
+                self.out.push(I::Call(index));
+                let ret = layout.rets[callee];
+                self.result(*dst, ret);
+            }
+            Stmt::CallRuntime { dst, func, args } => {
+                let index = *self
+                    .layout
+                    .hosts
+                    .get(func)
+                    .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("`{}` is not a host function", func.symbol()))?;
+                for a in args {
+                    self.get(*a);
+                }
+                self.out.push(I::Call(index));
+                self.result(*dst, func.signature().1);
+                if *func == RtFunc::Panic {
+                    self.out.push(I::Unreachable);
+                }
+            }
+            Stmt::Mem { dst, op, args } => self.mem(*dst, *op, args)?,
+            Stmt::Await { .. } => bail!(
+                "an `.await` marker reached codegen: `nova-mir`'s async state-machine \
+                 transform must split every `async fn` body at its await points first"
+            ),
+            other => bail!("not lowered yet: {other:?}"),
+        }
+        Ok(())
+    }
+
+    /// A memory access in line (spec §5.1).
+    fn mem(&mut self, dst: Option<Temp>, op: MemOp, args: &[Temp]) -> Result<()> {
+        let arg = |i: usize| -> Result<Temp> {
+            args.get(i)
+                .copied()
+                .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("{op:?} takes more arguments"))
+        };
+        match op {
+            MemOp::Load8 => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.out.push(I::I64Load8U(mem(0, 0)));
+            }
+            MemOp::Load64 => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.out.push(I::I64Load(mem(0, 3)));
+            }
+            MemOp::Store8 => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.get(arg(1)?);
+                self.out.push(I::I64Store8(mem(0, 0)));
+            }
+            MemOp::Store64 => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.get(arg(1)?);
+                self.out.push(I::I64Store(mem(0, 3)));
+            }
+            MemOp::Copy => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.wrap(arg(1)?);
+                self.wrap(arg(2)?);
+                self.out.push(I::MemoryCopy {
+                    src_mem: 0,
+                    dst_mem: 0,
+                });
+            }
+            MemOp::Fill => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.wrap(arg(1)?);
+                self.wrap(arg(2)?);
+                self.out.push(I::MemoryFill(0));
+            }
+            MemOp::Pages => {
+                self.out.push(I::MemorySize(0));
+                self.out.push(I::I64ExtendI32U);
+            }
+            MemOp::Grow => {
+                self.wrap(arg(0)?);
+                self.out.push(I::MemoryGrow(0));
+                // -1, "cannot grow", stays -1.
+                self.out.push(I::I64ExtendI32S);
+            }
+            MemOp::HeapBase => self.out.push(I::I64Const(self.layout.heap_base)),
+        }
+        let produces = matches!(
+            op,
+            MemOp::Load8 | MemOp::Load64 | MemOp::Pages | MemOp::Grow | MemOp::HeapBase
+        );
+        if produces {
+            self.result(dst, MirTy::I64);
+        }
+        Ok(())
+    }
+}
+
+/// The instruction for `lhs op rhs`.
+fn bin(op: BinOp, class: OperandClass) -> Result<I<'static>> {
+    use BinOp::*;
+    Ok(match (class, op) {
+        (OperandClass::Float, Add) => I::F64Add,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Sub) => I::F64Sub,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Mul) => I::F64Mul,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Div) => I::F64Div,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Rem) => {
+            bail!("float remainder (%) is not supported yet by the WASM backend")
+        }
+        (OperandClass::Float, Eq) => I::F64Eq,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Ne) => I::F64Ne,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Lt) => I::F64Lt,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Le) => I::F64Le,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Gt) => I::F64Gt,
+        (OperandClass::Float, Ge) => I::F64Ge,
+        (OperandClass::Float, _) => bail!("bitwise operators are not defined for Float"),
+        (OperandClass::Bool, Eq) => I::I32Eq,
+        (OperandClass::Bool, Ne) => I::I32Ne,
+        (OperandClass::Bool, BitAnd) => I::I32And,
+        (OperandClass::Bool, BitOr) => I::I32Or,
+        (OperandClass::Bool, BitXor) => I::I32Xor,
+        (OperandClass::Bool, o) => bail!("operator {o:?} is not defined for Bool"),
+        (OperandClass::Int, Add) => I::I64Add,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Sub) => I::I64Sub,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Mul) => I::I64Mul,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Div) => I::I64DivS,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Rem) => I::I64RemS,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Eq) => I::I64Eq,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Ne) => I::I64Ne,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Lt) => I::I64LtS,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Le) => I::I64LeS,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Gt) => I::I64GtS,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Ge) => I::I64GeS,
+        (OperandClass::Int, BitAnd) => I::I64And,
+        (OperandClass::Int, BitOr) => I::I64Or,
+        (OperandClass::Int, BitXor) => I::I64Xor,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Shl) => I::I64Shl,
+        (OperandClass::Int, Shr) => I::I64ShrS,
+    })
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+    use nova_mir::{Block, BlockId};
+
+    fn func(name: &str, params: u32, temps: Vec<MirTy>, ret: MirTy, blocks: Vec<Block>) -> MirFn {
+        MirFn {
+            name: name.to_string(),
+            params,
+            takes_env: false,
+            capture_count: 0,
+            temps,
+            ret,
+            is_async: false,
+            blocks,
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn block(stmts: Vec<Stmt>, term: Terminator) -> Block {
+        Block { stmts, term }
+    }
+
+    fn module(functions: Vec<MirFn>) -> Module {
+        Module {
+            functions,
+            ..Default::default()
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Compile and validate, returning the module's bytes.
+    fn valid(m: &Module) -> Output {
+        let out = compile_module(m).expect("compiles");
+        wasmparser::Validator::new()
+            .validate_all(&out.wasm)
+            .expect("a valid module");
+        out
+    }
+
+    fn ret_unit() -> Terminator {
+        Terminator::Return(None)
+    }
+
+    /// Every scalar operator of each class.
+    #[test]
+    fn scalar_operators_lower_to_a_valid_module() {
+        use BinOp::*;
+        let mut stmts = vec![
+            Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(0), 7),
+            Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(1), 3),
+            Stmt::ConstFloat(Temp(2), 1.5),
+            Stmt::ConstBool(Temp(5), true),
+        ];
+        for op in [Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Rem, BitAnd, BitOr, BitXor, Shl, Shr] {
+            stmts.push(Stmt::Bin {
+                dst: Temp(0),
+                op,
+                class: OperandClass::Int,
+                lhs: Temp(0),
+                rhs: Temp(1),
+            });
+        }
+        for op in [Eq, Ne, Lt, Le, Gt, Ge] {
+            stmts.push(Stmt::Bin {
+                dst: Temp(5),
+                op,
+                class: OperandClass::Int,
+                lhs: Temp(0),
+                rhs: Temp(1),
+            });
+            stmts.push(Stmt::Bin {
+                dst: Temp(5),
+                op,
+                class: OperandClass::Float,
+                lhs: Temp(2),
+                rhs: Temp(2),
+            });
+        }
+        for op in [Add, Sub, Mul, Div] {
+            stmts.push(Stmt::Bin {
+                dst: Temp(2),
+                op,
+                class: OperandClass::Float,
+                lhs: Temp(2),
+                rhs: Temp(2),
+            });
+        }
+        stmts.extend([
+            Stmt::Neg {
+                dst: Temp(0),
+                class: OperandClass::Int,
+                src: Temp(0),
+            },
+            Stmt::Neg {
+                dst: Temp(2),
+                class: OperandClass::Float,
+                src: Temp(2),
+            },
+            Stmt::BitNot {
+                dst: Temp(0),
+                src: Temp(0),
+            },
+            Stmt::Not {
+                dst: Temp(5),
+                src: Temp(5),
+            },
+            Stmt::Bin {
+                dst: Temp(5),
+                op: Eq,
+                class: OperandClass::Bool,
+                lhs: Temp(5),
+                rhs: Temp(5),
+            },
+            Stmt::ConstUnit(Temp(3)),
+            Stmt::Copy {
+                dst: Temp(4),
+                src: Temp(0),
+            },
+        ]);
+        let temps = vec![
+            MirTy::I64,
+            MirTy::I64,
+            MirTy::F64,
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            MirTy::I64,
+            MirTy::I8,
+        ];
+        valid(&module(vec![func(
+            "main",
+            0,
+            temps,
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            vec![block(stmts, ret_unit())],
+        )]));
+    }
+
+    /// A call passes no `Unit` argument, and a result nobody keeps is
+    /// dropped.
+    #[test]
+    fn a_call_passes_no_unit_and_drops_an_unkept_result() {
+        let callee = func(
+            "f.1",
+            2,
+            vec![MirTy::Unit, MirTy::I64],
+            MirTy::I64,
+            vec![block(vec![], Terminator::Return(Some(Temp(1))))],
+        );
+        let main = func(
+            "main",
+            0,
+            vec![MirTy::Unit, MirTy::I64],
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            vec![block(
+                vec![
+                    Stmt::ConstUnit(Temp(0)),
+                    Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(1), 4),
+                    Stmt::Call {
+                        dst: None,
+                        callee: "f.1".to_string(),
+                        args: vec![Temp(0), Temp(1)],
+                    },
+                    Stmt::Call {
+                        dst: Some(Temp(1)),
+                        callee: "f.1".to_string(),
+                        args: vec![Temp(0), Temp(1)],
+                    },
+                ],
+                ret_unit(),
+            )],
+        );
+        valid(&module(vec![main, callee]));
+    }
+
+    /// `while i < 10 { if i == 5 { break } ; i = i + 1 }`, with a
+    /// `continue` path and a nested loop: the shapes MIR builds.
+    #[test]
+    fn loops_with_break_and_continue_are_structured() {
+        // 0: i = 0 → 1
+        // 1: (outer header) c = i < 10 → 2 | 6
+        // 2: c = i == 5 → 6 (break) | 3
+        // 3: (inner header) c = i < 3 → 4 | 5
+        // 4: i = i + 1 → 3 (continue inner)
+        // 5: i = i + 1 → 1 (continue outer)
+        // 6: return
+        let lt = |n| Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(2), n);
+        let cmp = |op| Stmt::Bin {
+            dst: Temp(1),
+            op,
+            class: OperandClass::Int,
+            lhs: Temp(0),
+            rhs: Temp(2),
+        };
+        let inc = Stmt::Bin {
+            dst: Temp(0),
+            op: BinOp::Add,
+            class: OperandClass::Int,
+            lhs: Temp(0),
+            rhs: Temp(3),
+        };
+        let br = |t, e| Terminator::Branch {
+            cond: Temp(1),
+            then_: BlockId(t),
+            else_: BlockId(e),
+        };
+        let blocks = vec![
+            block(
+                vec![Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(0), 0), Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(3), 1)],
+                Terminator::Goto(BlockId(1)),
+            ),
+            block(vec![lt(10), cmp(BinOp::Lt)], br(2, 6)),
+            block(vec![lt(5), cmp(BinOp::Eq)], br(6, 3)),
+            block(vec![lt(3), cmp(BinOp::Lt)], br(4, 5)),
+            block(vec![inc.clone_stmt()], Terminator::Goto(BlockId(3))),
+            block(vec![inc.clone_stmt()], Terminator::Goto(BlockId(1))),
+            block(vec![], ret_unit()),
+        ];
+        let f = func(
+            "main",
+            0,
+            vec![MirTy::I64, MirTy::I8, MirTy::I64, MirTy::I64],
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            blocks,
+        );
+        let cfg = structure::analyze(&f.blocks);
+        assert!(cfg.reducible);
+        valid(&module(vec![f]));
+    }
+
+    /// A dense `Switch` and a sparse one, each with a default.
+    #[test]
+    fn switches_are_structured() {
+        for arms in [vec![0, 1, 2], vec![-7, 40, 1000]] {
+            let mut blocks = vec![block(
+                vec![Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(0), 1)],
+                Terminator::Switch {
+                    disc: Temp(0),
+                    arms: arms
+                        .iter()
+                        .enumerate()
+                        .map(|(i, v)| (*v, BlockId(i as u32 + 1)))
+                        .collect(),
+                    default: BlockId(arms.len() as u32 + 1),
+                },
+            )];
+            for _ in 0..=arms.len() {
+                blocks.push(block(
+                    vec![],
+                    Terminator::Goto(BlockId(arms.len() as u32 + 2)),
+                ));
+            }
+            blocks.push(block(vec![], ret_unit()));
+            valid(&module(vec![func(
+                "main",
+                0,
+                vec![MirTy::I64],
+                MirTy::Unit,
+                blocks,
+            )]));
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Two entries into one cycle: the function falls back to a dispatch
+    /// loop, which still validates.
+    #[test]
+    fn an_irreducible_graph_falls_back_to_a_dispatch_loop() {
+        let blocks = vec![
+            block(
+                vec![Stmt::ConstBool(Temp(0), true)],
+                Terminator::Branch {
+                    cond: Temp(0),
+                    then_: BlockId(1),
+                    else_: BlockId(2),
+                },
+            ),
+            block(
+                vec![],
+                Terminator::Branch {
+                    cond: Temp(0),
+                    then_: BlockId(2),
+                    else_: BlockId(3),
+                },
+            ),
+            block(vec![], Terminator::Goto(BlockId(1))),
+            block(vec![], ret_unit()),
+        ];
+        let f = func("main", 0, vec![MirTy::I8], MirTy::Unit, blocks);
+        assert!(!structure::analyze(&f.blocks).reducible);
+        let out = valid(&module(vec![f]));
+        let has_br_table = wasmparser::Parser::new(0)
+            .parse_all(&out.wasm)
+            .filter_map(Result::ok)
+            .any(|p| match p {
+                wasmparser::Payload::CodeSectionEntry(body) => body
+                    .get_operators_reader()
+                    .map(|ops| {
+                        ops.into_iter()
+                            .filter_map(Result::ok)
+                            .any(|op| matches!(op, wasmparser::Operator::BrTable { .. }))
+                    })
+                    .unwrap_or(false),
+                _ => false,
+            });
+        assert!(has_br_table, "the fallback dispatches through a br_table");
+    }
+
+    /// Every memory builtin, in line.
+    #[test]
+    fn memory_accesses_lower_in_line() {
+        let a = |op, args: Vec<u32>, dst: Option<u32>| Stmt::Mem {
+            dst: dst.map(Temp),
+            op,
+            args: args.into_iter().map(Temp).collect(),
+        };
+        let stmts = vec![
+            Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(0), 64),
+            Stmt::ConstInt(Temp(1), 9),
+            a(MemOp::Store64, vec![0, 1], None),
+            a(MemOp::Load64, vec![0], Some(1)),
+            a(MemOp::Store8, vec![0, 1], None),
+            a(MemOp::Load8, vec![0], Some(1)),
+            a(MemOp::Copy, vec![0, 0, 1], None),
+            a(MemOp::Fill, vec![0, 1, 1], None),
+            a(MemOp::Pages, vec![], Some(1)),
+            a(MemOp::Grow, vec![1], Some(1)),
+            a(MemOp::HeapBase, vec![], Some(1)),
+        ];
+        valid(&module(vec![func(
+            "main",
+            0,
+            vec![MirTy::I64, MirTy::I64],
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            vec![block(stmts, ret_unit())],
+        )]));
+    }
+
+    /// A host function is imported from `env` once, under its field name.
+    #[test]
+    fn a_host_function_is_imported_from_env() {
+        let call = |func| Stmt::CallRuntime {
+            dst: None,
+            func,
+            args: vec![Temp(0)],
+        };
+        let f = func(
+            "main",
+            0,
+            vec![MirTy::Ptr],
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            vec![block(
+                vec![
+                    call(RtFunc::Println),
+                    call(RtFunc::Print),
+                    call(RtFunc::Println),
+                ],
+                ret_unit(),
+            )],
+        );
+        let out = valid(&module(vec![f]));
+        assert_eq!(out.imports, ["println", "print"]);
+    }
+
+    /// A runtime function `std/rt` implements must have become a call to
+    /// it; one WASM lacks must have been refused already (E0091).
+    #[test]
+    fn a_runtime_function_that_is_not_the_shims_is_refused() {
+        for (func, says) in [
+            (
+                RtFunc::StrConcat,
+                "MIR must call `std/rt`'s `rt_str_concat`",
+            ),
+            (RtFunc::TaskBlockOn, "which has no such function"),
+        ] {
+            let f = |func| {
+                func_with(
+                    "main",
+                    vec![block(
+                        vec![Stmt::CallRuntime {
+                            dst: None,
+                            func,
+                            args: vec![],
+                        }],
+                        ret_unit(),
+                    )],
+                )
+            };
+            let e = compile_module(&module(vec![f(func)])).expect_err("refused");
+            assert!(e.to_string().contains(says), "{e}");
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn func_with(name: &str, blocks: Vec<Block>) -> MirFn {
+        func(name, 0, vec![], MirTy::Unit, blocks)
+    }
+
+    /// `%` on `Float`s is refused, as the native backends refuse it.
+    #[test]
+    fn float_remainder_is_refused() {
+        let f = func(
+            "main",
+            0,
+            vec![MirTy::F64],
+            MirTy::Unit,
+            vec![block(
+                vec![
+                    Stmt::ConstFloat(Temp(0), 1.0),
+                    Stmt::Bin {
+                        dst: Temp(0),
+                        op: BinOp::Rem,
+                        class: OperandClass::Float,
+                        lhs: Temp(0),
+                        rhs: Temp(0),
+                    },
+                ],
+                ret_unit(),
+            )],
+        );
+        let e = compile_module(&module(vec![f])).expect_err("refused");
+        assert!(e.to_string().contains("float remainder (%)"), "{e}");
+    }
+
+    trait CloneStmt {
+        fn clone_stmt(&self) -> Stmt;
+    }
+
+    impl CloneStmt for Stmt {
+        fn clone_stmt(&self) -> Stmt {
+            match self {
+                Stmt::Bin {
+                    dst,
+                    op,
+                    class,
+                    lhs,
+                    rhs,
+                } => Stmt::Bin {
+                    dst: *dst,
+                    op: *op,
+                    class: *class,
+                    lhs: *lhs,
+                    rhs: *rhs,
+                },
+                other => panic!("not cloned: {other:?}"),
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}
diff --git a/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/structure.rs b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/structure.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..71c27d4
--- /dev/null
+++ b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/structure.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,256 @@
+//! The shape of a function's control flow, for structuring it (spec
+//! `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`
+//! §4.2): reverse postorder, dominators, merge nodes and loop headers, as
+//! Ramsey's "Beyond Relooper" (ICFP 2022) uses them. A graph is reducible
+//! when every back edge's target dominates its source; only then can it be
+//! nested as `block`s and `loop`s, and otherwise the caller falls back to a
+//! dispatch loop.
+
+use nova_mir::{Block, Terminator};
+
+/// No reverse-postorder number: the block is unreachable from the entry.
+pub(crate) const UNREACHED: usize = usize::MAX;
+
+/// A function's blocks, analysed.
+#[derive(Debug)]
+pub(crate) struct Cfg {
+    /// The reachable blocks, in reverse postorder from block 0. Only the
+    /// tests read it; lowering goes by [`Cfg::rpo_index`].
+    #[allow(dead_code)]
+    pub rpo: Vec<usize>,
+    /// Each block's place in [`Cfg::rpo`], or [`UNREACHED`].
+    pub rpo_index: Vec<usize>,
+    /// Each block's immediate dominator (block 0's is itself), or
+    /// [`UNREACHED`]. Only the tests read it; lowering goes by
+    /// [`Cfg::children`].
+    #[allow(dead_code)]
+    pub idom: Vec<usize>,
+    /// Each block's children in the dominator tree, in reverse postorder.
+    pub children: Vec<Vec<usize>>,
+    /// Whether two or more forward edges enter the block.
+    pub merge: Vec<bool>,
+    /// Whether a back edge enters the block.
+    pub loop_header: Vec<bool>,
+    /// Whether every back edge's target dominates its source.
+    pub reducible: bool,
+}
+
+/// The blocks `term` can go to, once per edge.
+pub(crate) fn successors(term: &Terminator) -> Vec<usize> {
+    match term {
+        Terminator::Goto(b) => vec![b.0 as usize],
+        Terminator::Branch { then_, else_, .. } => vec![then_.0 as usize, else_.0 as usize],
+        Terminator::Switch { arms, default, .. } => arms
+            .iter()
+            .map(|(_, b)| b.0 as usize)
+            .chain([default.0 as usize])
+            .collect(),
+        Terminator::Return(_) | Terminator::Trap => Vec::new(),
+    }
+}
+
+pub(crate) fn analyze(blocks: &[Block]) -> Cfg {
+    let n = blocks.len();
+    let succs: Vec<Vec<usize>> = blocks.iter().map(|b| successors(&b.term)).collect();
+
+    // Postorder by an explicit stack: a function may have thousands of
+    // blocks, and recursion would bound that by this thread's stack.
+    let mut visited = vec![false; n];
+    let mut post = Vec::with_capacity(n);
+    let mut stack: Vec<(usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
+    if n > 0 {
+        visited[0] = true;
+        stack.push((0, 0));
+    }
+    while let Some(&(b, i)) = stack.last() {
+        if let Some(&s) = succs[b].get(i) {
+            if let Some(top) = stack.last_mut() {
+                top.1 += 1;
+            }
+            if !visited[s] {
+                visited[s] = true;
+                stack.push((s, 0));
+            }
+        } else {
+            post.push(b);
+            stack.pop();
+        }
+    }
+    let rpo: Vec<usize> = post.into_iter().rev().collect();
+    let mut rpo_index = vec![UNREACHED; n];
+    for (i, &b) in rpo.iter().enumerate() {
+        rpo_index[b] = i;
+    }
+
+    let mut preds: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); n];
+    for &b in &rpo {
+        for &s in &succs[b] {
+            preds[s].push(b);
+        }
+    }
+
+    // Cooper, Harvey and Kennedy, "A Simple, Fast Dominance Algorithm".
+    let mut idom = vec![UNREACHED; n];
+    if n > 0 {
+        idom[0] = 0;
+    }
+    let intersect = |idom: &[usize], mut a: usize, mut b: usize| {
+        while a != b {
+            while rpo_index[a] > rpo_index[b] {
+                a = idom[a];
+            }
+            while rpo_index[b] > rpo_index[a] {
+                b = idom[b];
+            }
+        }
+        a
+    };
+    let mut changed = true;
+    while changed {
+        changed = false;
+        for &b in rpo.iter().skip(1) {
+            let mut new = UNREACHED;
+            for &p in &preds[b] {
+                if idom[p] == UNREACHED {
+                    continue;
+                }
+                new = if new == UNREACHED {
+                    p
+                } else {
+                    intersect(&idom, p, new)
+                };
+            }
+            if idom[b] != new {
+                idom[b] = new;
+                changed = true;
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    let mut children: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); n];
+    for &b in rpo.iter().skip(1) {
+        children[idom[b]].push(b);
+    }
+    for c in &mut children {
+        c.sort_by_key(|&b| rpo_index[b]);
+    }
+
+    let dominates = |a: usize, mut b: usize| loop {
+        if a == b {
+            return true;
+        }
+        if b == 0 {
+            return false;
+        }
+        b = idom[b];
+    };
+    let mut forward_in = vec![0usize; n];
+    let mut loop_header = vec![false; n];
+    let mut reducible = true;
+    for &b in &rpo {
+        for &s in &succs[b] {
+            if rpo_index[s] <= rpo_index[b] {
+                loop_header[s] = true;
+                if !dominates(s, b) {
+                    reducible = false;
+                }
+            } else {
+                forward_in[s] += 1;
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    let merge = forward_in.iter().map(|&k| k >= 2).collect();
+
+    Cfg {
+        rpo,
+        rpo_index,
+        idom,
+        children,
+        merge,
+        loop_header,
+        reducible,
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+    use nova_mir::{BlockId, Temp};
+
+    fn block(term: Terminator) -> Block {
+        Block {
+            stmts: Vec::new(),
+            term,
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn goto(b: u32) -> Terminator {
+        Terminator::Goto(BlockId(b))
+    }
+
+    fn branch(t: u32, e: u32) -> Terminator {
+        Terminator::Branch {
+            cond: Temp(0),
+            then_: BlockId(t),
+            else_: BlockId(e),
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// `if`/`else` joining: 0 branches to 1 and 2, both go to 3.
+    #[test]
+    fn a_diamond_has_one_merge_node_and_no_loop() {
+        let cfg = analyze(&[
+            block(branch(1, 2)),
+            block(goto(3)),
+            block(goto(3)),
+            block(Terminator::Return(None)),
+        ]);
+        assert!(cfg.reducible);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.merge, [false, false, false, true]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.loop_header, [false; 4]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.idom, [0, 0, 0, 0]);
+        // Depth first from 0 reaches 1, then 3, then 2: reverse postorder is
+        // 0, 2, 1, 3.
+        assert_eq!(cfg.rpo, [0, 2, 1, 3]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.children[0], [2, 1, 3]);
+    }
+
+    /// `while`: 0 → 1 (header) → 2 (body) → 1, and 1 → 3 (exit).
+    #[test]
+    fn a_while_loop_has_a_header() {
+        let cfg = analyze(&[
+            block(goto(1)),
+            block(branch(2, 3)),
+            block(goto(1)),
+            block(Terminator::Return(None)),
+        ]);
+        assert!(cfg.reducible);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.loop_header, [false, true, false, false]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.merge, [false; 4]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.idom, [0, 0, 1, 1]);
+    }
+
+    /// Two entries into one cycle, 1 ⇄ 2, from 0: no node of the cycle
+    /// dominates the other.
+    #[test]
+    fn a_cycle_with_two_entries_is_irreducible() {
+        let cfg = analyze(&[block(branch(1, 2)), block(goto(2)), block(goto(1))]);
+        assert!(!cfg.reducible);
+    }
+
+    /// A block nothing reaches has no number, and no dominator.
+    #[test]
+    fn an_unreached_block_is_left_out() {
+        let cfg = analyze(&[block(Terminator::Return(None)), block(goto(0))]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.rpo, [0]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.rpo_index[1], UNREACHED);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.idom[1], UNREACHED);
+    }
+
+    /// The same target twice from one block is two edges, so a merge.
+    #[test]
+    fn two_edges_to_one_block_make_it_a_merge_node() {
+        let cfg = analyze(&[block(branch(1, 1)), block(Terminator::Return(None))]);
+        assert_eq!(cfg.merge, [false, true]);
+    }
+}
