````diff
+            "infinityy",
+            "nanx",
+            "in",
+            "∞",
+            "١٢",
+            "1,5",
+        ];
+        let literal = |s: &str| {
+            let escaped: String = s
+                .chars()
+                .map(|c| format!("\\u{{{:x}}}", u32::from(c)))
+                .collect();
+            format!("\"{escaped}\"")
+        };
+        let list: Vec<String> = cases.iter().map(|s| literal(s)).collect();
+        let script = format!(
+            "{FLOAT}\nconst view = new DataView(new ArrayBuffer(8));\n\
+             for (const s of [{}]) {{\n  const v = floatFrom(s);\n  view.setFloat64(0, v);\n  \
+             console.log(Number.isNaN(v) ? \"NaN\" : view.getBigUint64(0).toString(16));\n}}\n",
+            list.join(", ")
+        );
+        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("nova-codegen-wasm-float-from");
+        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect("a scratch directory");
+        let file = dir.join("float_from.js");
+        std::fs::write(&file, script).expect("the script");
+        let out = node.arg(&file).output().expect("node runs");
+        assert!(
+            out.status.success(),
+            "{}",
+            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)
+        );
+        let got = String::from_utf8(out.stdout).expect("UTF-8");
+        let got: Vec<&str> = got.lines().collect();
+        assert_eq!(got.len(), cases.len(), "{got:?}");
+        for (case, got) in cases.iter().zip(got) {
+            let v = case.parse::<f64>().unwrap_or(0.0);
+            let expected = if v.is_nan() {
+                "NaN".to_string()
+            } else {
+                format!("{:x}", v.to_bits())
+            };
+            assert_eq!(got, expected, "floatFrom({case:?})");
+        }
+    }
 }
diff --git a/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/shim/float.js b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/shim/float.js
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..e02ce3f
--- /dev/null
+++ b/crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/shim/float.js
@@ -0,0 +1,97 @@
+
+// `Float` to and from text as nova-runtime gives it, which is Rust's
+// (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md
+// §6). Ties are decided exactly, on the double's bits.
+
+// |v| exactly, as [m, e]: m × 2^e, m a BigInt.
+function exactly(v) {
+  const view = new DataView(new ArrayBuffer(8));
+  view.setFloat64(0, v);
+  const bits = view.getBigUint64(0);
+  const biased = Number((bits >> 52n) & 0x7ffn);
+  const fraction = bits & 0xfffffffffffffn;
+  return biased === 0 ? [fraction, -1074] : [fraction | 0x10000000000000n, biased - 1075];
+}
+
+// The digits and exponent of JS's shortest text for a positive double: D ×
+// 10^q, D a BigInt with no trailing zero.
+function shortest(a) {
+  const [mantissa, exp] = String(a).split("e");
+  const [whole, fraction = ""] = mantissa.split(".");
+  let digits = (whole + fraction).replace(/^0+/, "");
+  let q = (exp === undefined ? 0 : Number(exp)) - fraction.length;
+  while (digits.length > 1 && digits.endsWith("0")) {
+    digits = digits.slice(0, -1);
+    q += 1;
+  }
+  return [BigInt(digits), q];
+}
+
+// D × 10^q written out, with no exponent.
+function plain(d, q) {
+  const digits = d.toString();
+  if (q >= 0) return digits + "0".repeat(q);
+  const point = digits.length + q;
+  return point > 0
+    ? `${digits.slice(0, point)}.${digits.slice(point)}`
+    : `0.${"0".repeat(-point)}${digits}`;
+}
+
+// Rust's `Display` for f64: the shortest digits that read back as `v`,
+// never an exponent. Where two shortest texts are equally near, ECMAScript
+// takes the even one and Rust the larger: so when JS's last digit is even
+// and |v| lies exactly halfway to the next, take the next.
+function floatText(v) {
+  if (Number.isNaN(v)) return "NaN";
+  if (v === Infinity) return "inf";
+  if (v === -Infinity) return "-inf";
+  if (v === 0) return Object.is(v, -0) ? "-0" : "0";
+  const a = Math.abs(v);
+  let [d, q] = shortest(a);
+  if (d % 2n === 0n) {
+    const [m, e] = exactly(a);
+    let twice = 2n * m;
+    let halfway = 2n * d + 1n;
+    if (e >= 0) twice <<= BigInt(e);
+    else halfway <<= BigInt(-e);
+    if (q >= 0) halfway *= 10n ** BigInt(q);
+    else twice *= 10n ** BigInt(-q);
+    if (twice === halfway && Number(`${d + 1n}e${q}`) === a) d += 1n;
+  }
+  return (v < 0 ? "-" : "") + plain(d, q);
+}
+
+// Rust's `{:.N}` for f64, `places` clamped to 0..=17: the exact value
+// rounded half to even. The sign stays, on -0.0 too.
+function floatFixed(v, places) {
+  if (Number.isNaN(v)) return "NaN";
+  if (v === Infinity) return "inf";
+  if (v === -Infinity) return "-inf";
+  const p = Math.min(Math.max(Number(places), 0), 17);
+  const [m, e] = exactly(Math.abs(v));
+  let n;
+  if (e >= 0) {
+    n = (m << BigInt(e)) * 10n ** BigInt(p);
+  } else {
+    const num = m * 10n ** BigInt(p);
+    const den = 1n << BigInt(-e);
+    n = num / den;
+    const twice = 2n * (num % den);
+    if (twice > den || (twice === den && n % 2n === 1n)) n += 1n;
+  }
+  const digits = n.toString().padStart(p + 1, "0");
+  const body = p > 0 ? `${digits.slice(0, -p)}.${digits.slice(-p)}` : digits;
+  return (v < 0 || Object.is(v, -0) ? "-" : "") + body;
+}
+
+// Rust's `str::parse::<f64>`, or 0.0 where it fails (nova_rt_str_to_float's
+// `unwrap_or(0.0)`): its grammar exactly, then `Number`, which rounds
+// correctly as Rust does.
+const FLOAT_TEXT = /^[+-]?(?:inf|infinity|nan|(?:\d+\.?\d*|\.\d+)(?:e[+-]?\d+)?)$/i;
+function floatFrom(s) {
+  if (!FLOAT_TEXT.test(s)) return 0;
+  const body = s.replace(/^[+-]/, "").toLowerCase();
+  if (body === "inf" || body === "infinity") return s.startsWith("-") ? -Infinity : Infinity;
+  if (body === "nan") return NaN;
+  return Number(s);
+}
````

````diff
 ## [Unreleased]
 
+### Added
+
+- **`nova build --target wasm`, and its alias `nova bundle`,** compile a
+  program to WebAssembly: `dist/` holds `index.html`, `app.js` and
+  `app.wasm`, which run in a browser, or under Node with `node
+  dist/app.js`. A program may use the whole language and std's core,
+  bytes, collections, strings, fmt and json; hello world is about 2 KB
+  gzipped. A panic exits with status 134 and a trap with 132 under Node,
+  as natively (ADR 0039).
+- **`nova check --target wasm`** checks a program against WASM's std and
+  rules: an `extern` block is E0090, and a program that needs what WASM
+  lacks, such as `async fn main`, is E0091.
+
+### Changed
+
+- **`nova-codegen-wasm` is published** with the next release: 20 crates.
+

diff --git a/docs/adr/0038-phase-4-scope.md b/docs/adr/0038-phase-4-scope.md

@@ -0,0 +1,93 @@
+# ADR 0038 — Phase 4's scope, and what the phase leaves out
+
+## Status
+
+Accepted, 2026-10-14 (Phase 4.0, branch `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`;
+spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`).
+The decisions are the user's, made on 2026-10-14 (`docs/phase-4-plan.md`
+§1, and §3 decision 13).
+
+## Context
+
+`nova-spec/00-MASTER-SPEC.md` §3 lists Phase 4, "Frontend / WASM", in nine
+positions:
+
+1. a WASM backend, on `wasm-encoder` and `walrus`;
+2. a bundler, with tree shaking and code splitting;
+3. `std/dom`, bindings generated from `web-sys` descriptors;
+4. `std/ui`: signals, effects, memos and components;
+5. `std/ui/html`, element builders;
+6. `std/ui/router`;
+7. `nova dev`, a dev server with HMR;
+8. SSR, rendering natively and hydrating in WASM;
+9. SSG.
+
+Its gate: "`examples/06-counter-spa` and `examples/07-fullstack-blog` work
+in browser, pass Lighthouse 95+."
+
+`nova-spec/30-FRONTEND.md` describes more than the gate needs: examples 08
+to 10 (§12), code splitting with `lazy()` (§7.3), JS interop through npm
+(§9), source maps (§8), and `axum` for the dev server (§8).
+`nova-spec/60-EXAMPLES.md` §7 asks the blog for SQLite, and §8 calls
+examples 08 to 10 "post-Phase 6", contradicting `30-FRONTEND.md` §12.
+
+## Decision
+
+1. **Phase 4 builds what the gate's two examples need:** all nine
+   positions, each as far as the gate needs it.
+2. **It departs from the specs where the user decided so:**
+   - **Component syntax is built as `30-FRONTEND.md` §3 writes it:**
+     `component`, `view { … }`, named arguments and trailing blocks, with
+     an RFC.
+   - **New crates: `wasm-encoder` only,** with `wasmparser` as a
+     dev-dependency that validates what tests build. There is no `walrus`.
+     `nova dev` is written on `std::net`, without `axum` or `tokio`.
+   - **`nova dev` reloads the page** through Server-Sent Events, with an
+     overlay for compile errors. It is not HMR that keeps state.
+   - **The WASM runtime is the backend's own (route (a))**, written in Nova
+     on memory builtins, with the browser's work done in a JS shim. ADR
+     0039 records it.
+   - **The WASM collector is mark-sweep between event turns,** not the
+     reference counting `13-RUNTIME.md` §8 names. 4.1's ADR records it.
+   - **The blog stores its posts in a JSON file,** through `std/fs` and
+     `std/json`, not SQLite.
+   - **The gate is measured** by a CI job (Lighthouse in headless Chrome,
+     the gzipped sizes, Playwright) and by the user, who opens both
+     examples in their own browser at the phase's close.
+3. **Phase 4 leaves out:**
+   - examples 08 to 10;
+   - code splitting with `lazy()`;
+   - npm resolution for `@js_import`, and user-level `@js_import` and
+     `@js_export`. `std/dom` reaches the shim through compiler-known
+     functions instead;
+   - source maps (`30-FRONTEND.md` §8; `14-CODEGEN.md` §9.2);
+   - HMR that keeps state, `axum`, and `walrus`;
+   - bindings generated from `web-sys` descriptors: `std/dom` is a written
+     subset;
+   - reference counting, and the WASM GC proposal;
+   - `wasm-bindgen-test`, and visual regression snapshots
+     (`30-FRONTEND.md` §13);
+   - the LSP rows `40-TOOLING.md` §3.1 marks Phase 4 (inlay hints, code
+     lens, call hierarchy);
+   - SQLite.
+4. **A sub-phase's spec may pull one of these back in,** with a ruling that
+   names this ADR.
+
+## Consequences
+
+- **The master spec's Phase 4 list is not amended;** this ADR records the
+  difference, as ADR 0026 did for Phase 3.
+- **No new runtime dependency reaches an installed `nova` but
+  `wasm-encoder`** and its one dependency, `leb128fmt`.
+- **A program's JS dependencies wait** for the npm interop this ADR
+  leaves out.
+- **The examples beyond the gate,** and the LSP rows, go to the backlog the
+  phase's closing ADR keeps.
+
+## References
+
+- `nova-spec/00-MASTER-SPEC.md` §3, Phase 4.
+- `nova-spec/30-FRONTEND.md`, `nova-spec/60-EXAMPLES.md` §6 to §8,
+  `nova-spec/13-RUNTIME.md` §8, `nova-spec/40-TOOLING.md` §3.1.
+- `docs/phase-4-plan.md` §1, and §3 decisions 1, 3, 9, 11, 12 and 13.
+- ADR 0039, the WASM backend and its runtime.
diff --git a/docs/adr/0039-the-wasm-backend.md b/docs/adr/0039-the-wasm-backend.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..5936829
--- /dev/null
+++ b/docs/adr/0039-the-wasm-backend.md
@@ -0,0 +1,107 @@
+# ADR 0039 — The WASM backend and its runtime
+
+## Status
+
+Accepted, 2026-10-14 (Phase 4.0, branch `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`;
+spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`).
+The route and the runtime's language are the user's decisions of
+2026-10-14 (`docs/phase-4-plan.md` §3, decision 1).
+
+## Context
+
+`nova-spec/14-CODEGEN.md` §8 sketches a WASM backend on `wasm-encoder`,
+with a bundler step that writes a JS shim. `nova-spec/13-RUNTIME.md` §8
+says a WASM build uses the browser's event loop "via wasm-bindgen", starts
+with reference counting, and reaches the DOM through bindings generated
+from `web-sys`.
+
+`nova-runtime` cannot run in a browser:
+- its collector finds roots by scanning the native stack;
+- its executor and IO wait by blocking;
+- its net, fs, signal and process modules call the operating system.
+
+MIR suits WASM: every heap value is a `Ptr`, objects are 8-byte slots,
+generics are monomorphized, and async is already stackless.
+
+A spike in scratch space compared two routes for the runtime, on one
+program:
+- **(a)** the backend writes the runtime into every module, and a JS shim
+  does what the browser does better. It came to 1,847 bytes gzipped.
+- **(b)** `nova-runtime`'s OS-free parts, compiled for
+  `wasm32-unknown-unknown` ahead of time and committed. It came to 9,931
+  bytes gzipped, and such a module ships every runtime function in every
+  app: `str::parse::<f64>` alone added 14.6 KB. Its bytes depended on the
+  toolchain: Rust 1.78 built 24,917 bytes where 1.97 built 18,707.
+
+## Decision
+
+1. **Route (a).** `nova-codegen-wasm` lowers MIR to one module with
+   `wasm-encoder` 0.243.0, the last release that declares a minimum Rust
+   under the workspace's 1.78. `wasmparser` 0.243.0 validates, in tests
+   only. An installed `nova` needs no other toolchain to build for WASM.
+2. **The runtime is written in Nova:** `std/rt`, compiled only for the
+   WASM target.
+   - It is written on nine memory builtins (`mem_load8`, `mem_store8`,
+     `mem_load64`, `mem_store64`, `mem_copy`, `mem_fill`, `mem_pages`,
+     `mem_grow`, `heap_base`). They exist only in a WASM build's std, and
+     lower to instructions in line.
+   - Each of its functions is the port of a `nova-runtime` function, with
+     the same results, on `Int` addresses.
+   - MIR calls `std/rt`'s function where a native build calls
+     `nova-runtime`, so mono adds only the runtime functions a program
+     reaches. The backend converts between a `Ptr` and an `Int` address at
+     those calls.
+   - In 4.0 its allocator only grows. 4.1 adds the collector.
+3. **The shim does what JS does best:** printing, the panic, `Float` to
+   text and back, case mapping, and the hash seed.
+   - Each is held to `nova-runtime`'s result. ECMAScript breaks an exact
+     tie between two shortest texts of a `Float` toward the even digit, and
+     Rust upward; `toFixed` rounds ties up, and Rust to even. The shim
+     decides both exactly, with `BigInt` on the double's bits.
+   - Offline, against Rust: 2,005,144 doubles' text, 565,134 fixed-precision
+     cases and 399,831 parses, with no difference. Differential programs in
+     `tests/wasm/` keep it so. `str_to_float` is std's alone, so no program
+     can hand it any text: a unit test runs the shim's parser under Node
+     against Rust's `str::parse`, on every case of its grammar.
+   - Case mapping rests on the browser's Unicode tables, which can lag
+     Rust's.
+4. **Values and memory.**
+   - `I64` → `i64`, `F64` → `f64`, `I8` → `i32`, `Ptr` → `i32`.
+   - One 32-bit memory, 16 MB at first and at most 1 GB.
+   - Address 0 is never an object; `8..64` holds `std/rt`'s state words; the
+     data segment starts at 64.
+   - Slots stay 8 bytes, a `Ptr` zero-extended in its slot.
+   - A string literal is a static header in the data segment, not an
+     allocation per evaluation.
+5. **Control flow** is structured by Ramsey's algorithm ("Beyond
+   Relooper", 2022), for the reducible graphs MIR builds. An irreducible
+   graph falls back to a dispatch loop. A `Switch` is a chain of
+   comparisons.
+6. **Function values** are indices into one `funcref` table, called with
+   `call_indirect`.
+7. **`nova build --target wasm`,** and its alias `nova bundle`, write
+   `dist/`: `index.html`, `app.js` and `app.wasm`.
+8. **Under Node,** a panic exits with 134 and a trap with 132, the native
+   abort's and trap's statuses. Tests run WASM builds under Node, which CI
+   installs on all three systems; with `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1` a test fails
+   without it.
+
+## Consequences
+
+- **`nova-codegen-wasm` is published** from the next release: 20 crates.
+- **Two copies of each runtime function exist,** Rust's and Nova's. The
+  differential programs, and the golden suite run both ways, are what keep
+  them equal.
+- **A WASM build's recursion is bounded by V8's stack,** about 1 MB under
+  Node's default. A program that recurses that deep stops with `nova:
+  stack overflow`.
+- **The DOM, the event loop and the collector** follow in 4.1 and 4.2, on
+  this runtime.
+
+## References
+
+- `docs/phase-4-plan.md` §3, decisions 1 to 5.
+- The spec named above, §2, §4 to §6 and §13.
+- `nova-spec/14-CODEGEN.md` §2.3 and §8; `nova-spec/13-RUNTIME.md` §8;
+  `nova-spec/30-FRONTEND.md` §7 and §10.
+- ADR 0038, Phase 4's scope.
diff --git a/docs/phase-4-plan.md b/docs/phase-4-plan.md
index bf0bc55..9c8971c 100644
--- a/docs/phase-4-plan.md
+++ b/docs/phase-4-plan.md
@@ -422,6 +422,14 @@ pass, a PR, and a merge on the user's word.
     all three operating systems.
   - Hello world is under 30 KB gzipped, checked by a test.
 
+**Amended 2026-10-14 (branch `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`):** by the
+user's decisions of 2026-10-14, 4.0 covers every MIR statement, without a
+collector, and its runtime is written in Nova (`std/rt`). Its gate is hello
+world and the `tests/runtime/` programs its spec lists: `02-fibonacci` reads
+`args()`, which WASM's std lacks. 4.1 keeps the collector, std's other modules,
+async and the full golden suite
+(`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md`).
+
 ### 4.1 — The language on WASM
 - **The rest of MIR:** records, sums, arrays, closures, generics, and
   async.

+**Amended 2026-10-14 (branch `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`):** a WASM
+build's runtime is `std/rt`, written in Nova on nine memory builtins that only
+that target's std sees. Each function is the port of a `nova-runtime` function,
+with the same results, and the JS shim does printing, `Float` text and case
+mapping, held to the same results. There is no `wasm-bindgen`. In 4.0 the
+allocator only grows; the collector is mark-sweep between event turns, not
+reference counting, by the user's decision (ADR 0038), and arrives in 4.1.
+`std/dom` is a written subset, in 4.2
+(`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-4-0-foundations-design.md` §5, §6;
+ADR 0039).
+

+shim provides printing, `Float` text and case mapping.

 ## 3. Calling Convention
@@ -224,6 +234,14 @@ Plus a `nova-bundler` step that:
 3. Tree-shakes

+**Amended 2026-10-14 (branch `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`):** as built in
+4.0, the backend writes the shim as well, from the host functions the module
+imports, and `nova-driver` writes `dist/`. Values map as `I64` → `i64`, `F64` →
+`f64`, `I8` → `i32` and `Ptr` → `i32`; slots stay 8 bytes; control flow is
+structured by Ramsey's "Beyond Relooper", with a dispatch loop for an
+irreducible graph; function values are `funcref` table indices. Tree shaking is
+monomorphization's. `nova-bundler` is still empty (ADR 0039).
+
 ---
````

### Task 10: Verification, the mutants, the review, the PR

- [ ] **Step 1: The full checks.** First, `df -h /` must show at least
  10G free; if not, remove scratch directories of earlier sub-phases.

  ```sh
  cargo fmt --all -- --check
  cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
  cargo run --locked -q -p nova-cli -- fmt --check std examples
  cargo build --locked --workspace
  CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast > "$P/full-40x.txt" 2>&1
  grep '^test result' "$P/full-40x.txt" | awk '{p += $4; f += $6; i += $8} END {print p, f, i}'
  grep ' FAILED$' "$P/full-40x.txt"
  ```

  Expected:
  - `cargo fmt` and `nova fmt` print nothing, and clippy ends with
    `Finished`, with no warning or error;
  - `2116 1 10`, against the baseline's `2022 1 10`: 94 new tests,
    64 in `wasm_tests`, 23 in `nova-codegen-wasm`, three each in
    `lower_tests` and `nova-resolver`, one in `nova-typeck`;
  - the one failure is Task 0's IPv6 test.
- [ ] **Step 2: The lockfile.** `git diff --numstat 62aed1d -- Cargo.lock`.
  Expected: `30	0	Cargo.lock`, the lines of Tasks 2 and 5.
- [ ] **Step 3: Rust 1.78.**

  ```sh
  RUSTFLAGS="-D warnings" CARGO_TARGET_DIR="$P/msrv-40" cargo +1.78 check --locked --workspace
  ```

  Expected: clean, in about a minute. Then `rm -rf "$P/msrv-40"`.
- [ ] **Step 4: The package gate,** as CI's `package` job runs it.

  ```sh
  bash .github/scripts/package-gate.sh "$P/pg-40"
  ```

  `$P/pg-40` must not exist beforehand. Expected:
  - the first line, `package-gate: 20 crates at 0.3.2, in …`;
  - the last, `package-gate: passed in <n> s`, about five minutes.

  Then `rm -rf "$P/pg-40"`.
- [ ] **Step 5: The mutants.** One at a time, each edit is made, its tests
  run, and the file restored with `git checkout -- <file>`.
  - Each edit replaces one exact text, which occurs once in its file.
  - Each must fail the tests named. A mutant that passes them is a gap:
    record it in the ledger, and write the missing test before the
    review.
  - At the end, `git status --short` prints nothing.

  `W` stands for `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test -p nova-cli --test
  wasm_tests`.

  1. **E0090 dropped.** `crates/nova-resolver/src/lib.rs`: the
     `if target == CompileTarget::Wasm {` just above
     `for m in &all[..std_start] {` becomes `if false {`.
     - Run `cargo test -p nova-resolver an_extern_block_is_e0090_on_wasm`,
       and `W check_for_wasm_refuses_an_extern_block`.
     - Expected: both fail. The first with `left: []`,
       `right: ["E0090"]`; the second with `Unexpected success`.
  2. **The memory builtins seeded natively too.** The same file: the
     `if target == CompileTarget::Wasm {` just above
     `for b in Builtin::WASM_ONLY {` becomes a bare `{`.
     - Run `cargo test -p nova-resolver the_memory_builtins_exist_only_in_a_wasm_builds_std`.
     - Expected: fails: `mem_load8` "must not resolve in native module
       1".
  3. **No conversion from `Ptr` to `Int` at a call.**
     `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs`: the line
     `(MirTy::Ptr, MirTy::I64) => self.out.push(I::I64ExtendI32U),` goes.
     - Run `cargo test -p nova-codegen-wasm a_call_into_std_rt_converts_between_ptr_and_int`.
     - Expected: fails, `type mismatch: expected i64, found i32`.
  4. **A backward branch leaves a block.** The same file, in `branch`:
     `self.br(Ctx::Loop(to))` becomes `self.br(Ctx::Block(to))`.
     - Run `cargo test -p nova-codegen-wasm loops_with_break_and_continue_are_structured`,
       and `W gate::break_continue`.
     - Expected: both fail. The first with `no enclosing label for
       Block(3)`; the second with `internal WASM codegen error (this is a
       compiler bug)`.
  5. **`rt_alloc` is no root.** `crates/nova-mir/src/mono.rs`: the two
     lines `mir.runtime_alloc = Some(mangle(def, name, &[]));` and
     `worklist.push((def, Vec::new()));` become `let _ = (def, name);`.
     - Run `cargo test -p nova-mir --test lower_tests a_wasm_build`.
     - Expected: two fail, with `the allocator is named` and `["main"]`.
  6. **E0091 never reported.** The same file: the line
     `diagnostics.extend(unavailable_on_wasm(&mir, main_span));` becomes
     `let _ = unavailable_on_wasm;` and `let _ = main_span;`.
     - Run `cargo test -p nova-mir --test lower_tests a_runtime_function_wasm_lacks_is_e0091`,
       and `W check_for_wasm_reports_a_runtime_function_wasm_lacks`.
     - Expected: both fail. The first lowers the module; the second
       reports `Unexpected success`.
  7. **`floatText` without the tie rule.**
     `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/shim/float.js`: the line
     ``if (twice === halfway && Number(`${d + 1n}e${q}`) === a) d += 1n;``
     goes.
     - Run `W float_text_matches_nova_runtime`.
     - Expected: fails "on WASM", with `1658206780088562.2` where the
       native build prints `1658206780088562.3`.
  8. **`floatFixed` by `toFixed`.** The same file: after
     `const p = Math.min(Math.max(Number(places), 0), 17);` comes
     `return v.toFixed(p);`.
     - Run `W float_fixed_matches_nova_runtime`.
     - Expected: fails "on WASM", with `0.13` where the native build
       prints `0.12` (0.125 to two places).
  9. **`rt_str_cmp`'s order reversed.** `std/rt/lib.nova`: in
     `rt_str_cmp`, `if x < y { return -1 }` and `if x > y { return 1 }`
     swap their results.
     - Run `W std_rt_strings_and_bytes_match_nova_runtime`.
     - Expected: fails "on WASM".
  10. **`--release` accepted for `wasm`.** `crates/nova-cli/src/cmd/run.rs`:
      the three lines of `if cmd.release { anyhow::bail!(…) }` in
      `build_wasm` go.
      - Run `W release_does_not_apply_to_wasm`.
      - Expected: fails, `Unexpected success`.
  11. **A project's `dist/` in the current directory.** The same file:
      `(None, Mode::Project { root, .. }) => root.join("dist"),` becomes
      `(None, Mode::Project { .. }) => PathBuf::from("dist"),`.
      - Run `W a_project_builds_into_dist_at_its_root`.
      - Expected: fails, at `dir.join("dist").join("app.wasm").is_file()`.
  12. **A literal's header points 8 bytes short.**
      `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/lib.rs`: `let bytes = header + 16;`
      becomes `let bytes = header + 8;`.
      - Run `W hello_world_runs_under_node`.
      - Expected: fails, printing `h\0\0\0\0\0\0\0Hello\n` for `Hello,
        World!\n`.
  13. **No routing after the async transform.**
      `crates/nova-mir/src/mono.rs`: the three lines
      `for f in &mut mir.functions { route_runtime(f, impls, module); }`
      after `crate::async_lower::transform(&mut mir);` go.
      - Run `cargo test -p nova-mir --test lower_tests a_runtime_function_wasm_lacks_is_e0091`.
      - Expected: fails. The E0091 list also holds ``this program needs
        `alloc` ``, from the transform's state objects.
  14. **`floatFrom` without the grammar.**
      `crates/nova-codegen-wasm/src/shim/float.js`: the line
      `if (!FLOAT_TEXT.test(s)) return 0;` goes.
      - Run `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test -p nova-codegen-wasm float_from_is_rusts_parse`.
      - Expected: fails at `floatFrom("+")`, with `NaN` where Rust's
        parse gives `0`.

- [ ] **Step 6: The size,** for the ledger. In Task 6's scratch hello
  world, rebuilt with this tree's `nova`:

  ```sh
  NOVA="$PWD/target/debug/nova"
  cd "$P/hello-40" && "$NOVA" build --target wasm main.nova
  cat dist/app.wasm dist/app.js | gzip -9 | wc -c
  cd - >/dev/null
  ```

  Expected: about 2,000. The prototype measured 1,963 bytes with
  `flate2`, as the test does; the limit is 30 KB.
- [ ] **Step 7: One fresh read-only review, then one fix pass** (the
  user's workflow).
  - The reviewer reads `git diff $BASE..HEAD` (Task 0) against the spec and
    this plan, and is told to hunt for:
    - wrong results under Node, unlike the native build's;
    - a module that would not validate;
    - a test that could pass without the code it names;
    - a lockfile line beyond the 30, and a published crate without its
      files;
    - a record that says what the code does not.
  - Each finding is fixed in one commit, `wasm: the review's fixes`, or
    answered in the ledger with the reason it stands.
  - After the fixes, Step 1 runs again.
- [ ] **Step 8: The PR.**
  - Push: `git push -u origin claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q`.
  - Open the PR to `main` with the repository's template headings, if it
    has one. The body names no local path and no person.
  - It holds:
    - what 4.0 adds;
    - the gate's evidence: hello world, the 46 programs, the size;
    - the decisions, numbered as here, with 1, 8, 18, 23, 24 and 25
      marked as differing from the spec;
    - the lockfile's 30 lines;
    - the mutants, and the review's findings and what came of each.
  - Read CI once it ends. Expected: green on all three systems. The test
    job installs Node 22 and sets `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1`, so the WASM
    tests run on Windows and macOS too, for the first time.
- [ ] **Step 9: The merge,** only on the user's word.
  - By rebase, pinned to the PR's head commit. Never auto-merge.
  - Then `git fetch origin main` and `git diff <head> origin/main
    --stat`, which prints nothing: the merged tree is the PR's.
